On Self-Manifestation · Engineering Chapter · The Field Concretization Group Form · Chapter 18 v1.0

# The Mathematical Form of Trace-Response Set Transformation

1\. Purpose

### 1.1  Use and Central Question

This paper is a Mathematical Form (principal form, S ↦ M; the specification of Mathematical Forms in Canon Chapter 1, Part Three, Chapter Two of the main book *On Self-Manifestation* v0.7.8). Its use: to set up, for an identified Trace-Response set, a system that represents and runs its transformation, so that the second component of the ontological definition, “the mode of organization of this set, transforming under the action of historical dynamics, and its activity”, together with the reading “Historical dynamics forms tendency; the carrying-out of tendency is activity; activity and tendency organizing (that is, organizing the set) is transformation, and it transforms according to tendency, as water seeks low ground” (*The Grading Form of Tendency Types* v1.0, Section 2.1, F2), becomes expressible, runnable, solvable and readable back on declared host models.  
The central question: \[S1\] proved that active concretization of a Sedimentation-Field on a declared instance requires three duties—Trace-Response set, organizational participation and historical dynamics—with Org(T) serving only as an argument of predicates; item 9 of its degree of answer reads “the general identity condition for modes of organization—unanswered”. \[S2\] gives a formal system in which qualification is a function of mechanism witnesses, with qualification readings as its output. \[S12\] represents D, B and F at the path layer. \[S9\] grades tendency directions only on share readings. \[17\] identifies Trace-Responses and organization from the object and adjudicates the history condition. None of these represents “how the set transforms”: what determines a given activity, which affected subset it rewrites, and how the rewritten set forms the next tendency. This paper answers that question.  
1.2  The Structure Formalized and Entry Conditions  
S₁₈ consists of the following established structures: the second component of *The Ontological Definition of the Sedimentation-Field* v2.0 and *The Ontological Structure Diagram of the Sedimentation-Field* v0.4 (Tᵢ, Sᵢ, Dᵢ, D′ᵢ, the set of dynamics types), with the delimitation of the Trace-Response in v0.4.1; Org(T), Cond, Act, HistCond, the component criterion, and maintenance and rest of \[S1\]; the two layers R and F and the witness-preserving embedding J of \[S2\]; Definitions 3, 4 and 9 and Equations 2 and 10 of *The Mathematical Form of the Field* v2.7; the tendency structure S of Definition 3 of *The General Form of Self-Manifestation-Field Induction*. For it this paper establishes S₁₈ ⟼ M₁₈.  
Entry conditions: each component of S₁₈ has been established by a proof principal form or by the main book (Theorem 1 and Corollary 1 of \[S1\]; *The Mathematical Form of the Field*; *The General Form of Self-Manifestation-Field Induction*) and has stable terminology. The formalization of organization and of tendency is a \[Representational choice\] of this paper (Section 3).

### 1.3  Division of Labor

The bridge from identification to adjudication belongs to \[17\]: the three-way division of Trace-Responses, depth bearing, the organization hypergraph, the effective-difference certificate for the history condition, and cross-record chains. This paper takes the results of that identification as input and studies how the set transforms and how it is run and solved; the adjudication theorems of \[17\] are cited as conclusions of a preceding paper. Tendency, as the inclination layer of the mode of organization, is stated by this paper (D18-6), and \[17\] uses it under the same criterion.

### 1.4  Boundary and Strength

Object domain: declared host models (finitely many Structural Positions or finitely many latent positions, rational parameters, finite windows, deterministic read-out and write); two representative declared models (a minimal model of the *Drosophila* mushroom-body type; a Tero-type flow network and the published model of Marbach et al. 2023) and several constructed classes (consolidation states, existence-variable chains, self-loop toys, general-boundary conductance networks). The conclusions are model conclusions (strength A); statements about real objects are at most conditional predictions (B), which must list their unverified premises; no empirical certification (C) and no new qualification verdicts are issued.

### 1.5  Composition of the Conclusions

　1\. Representation: five layers (complete mathematical state, source–identity record, labeled organization description, tendency, activity) and the running equations; tendency is defined on the signal class and precedes activity, and activity is the value of tendency at the actual signal; organization takes the labeled description as its object of computation; local write constrains the write before booking, and locality of the actual post-state additionally requires position-wise post-processing (D18-1–D18-14, Equations 4.1–4.4, Theorems 18-1 and 18-3).  
　2\. Identity: relabeling equivariance (under original-mechanism semantics it is given by commutation of the total write; under booked-as-effective semantics it additionally requires commutation of the filtered write, for which process pairing is a sufficient condition); the layering of the two kinds of identity, exact and up to relabeling, with a strictness witness for each of the seven arrows; isomorphism of connection graphs does not suffice for identity of organization (Theorem 18-2).  
　3\. Read-back: on the law class of \[S2\], “effective-difference certificate ⇒ numerical support”; the converse fails, and the separation falls on the effective write; HistCond\_F is the three-valued complete conjunction of D18 of \[S2\]; the three value columns are evidence summaries, and the main verdict combines the atoms; “effective difference certified” requires item-by-item certification of the four clauses of Definition 6′ of \[17\], with the difference and the certification bound to the same contrast in the declared contrast family, and an action refutation for the same event is not overridden by an external claim; entry into the F layer of \[S2\] via J₁₈ is split into three conditions: an F candidate event table can be generated, the numerical trajectory of the original run is reproduced, and witnesses and qualification are preserved (Theorem 18-4).  
　4\. Bearing of tendency: under local write, the entire action of the rest of the set on an element is borne by a single curve, the “tendency received by the position itself”, ψ\_x; \|F′\| \< 1 is sufficient, \> 1 is unstable, = 1 awaits higher order; the direction law; two layers, activity intervention and post-state intervention (Theorem 18-5); the relative-resistance criterion of Marbach et al. 2023 is a structural analogy (Theorem 18-6).  
　5\. Hidden change: a criterion for permanent hiding on a restricted class (including affine post-processing common to the two arms, whose linear part is absorbed into the difference recursion), and a Cayley–Hamilton decision on linear time-invariant closed loops (Theorem 18-7).  
　6\. Write laws rewritten by history: representation of law-layer Trace-Responses; the first component difference is kept apart from the difference in actual post-states, and both setting and weak writes take effect according to the actual recursion; an overwrite requires the complete mathematical states after overwriting to be identical; history control and clock control are discriminated in two arms with the same training, the same read-out and the same blockade (Theorem 18-8).  
　7\. Change of organizational relations: derived incidence and existence variables are represented within the run; the remote effect of a removal under fixed injection is ΔQ\_x = Q\_e·T\_x(u→v), replaced under fixed-potential and mixed boundaries by the incremental response T⁰ for the same boundary; when a bridge is disconnected, conservation is checked connected component by connected component (Theorem 18-9).  
　8\. Solvability (Theorem 18-10); for each link of the seven-step research procedure for concretized fields, what bears it, how it connects, its conditions of validity and its distortions (8.3); seven-step runs for two representatives, *Drosophila* and *Physarum polycephalum* (9.2, 9.3).

### 1.6  Terms and Notation

Wherever a Trace-Response set is meant, we write “organization”; “Group Form” refers only to the series of papers. In the ontological structure diagram, D′ᵢ acts again directly on the Trace-Response set Tᵢ₊₁ of the next moment.  
Citation marks: \[S1\] to \[S16\] denote Chapters 1 to 16 of the Field Concretization Group Form v1.0, \[S9\] being *The Grading Form of Tendency Types* v1.0 (main-text SHA-256 d1748ef1806e021fddddb8b307948a48255a88ef737d7d3712f55004acbd6b04); \[17\] denotes Chapter 17 of the Field Concretization Group Form, *The Adjudication Form for Identifying Concretized Fields* v1.0 (main-text SHA-256 cfd8a0806c37e5a7dd27529439b6d04673a96e16530ad26951c2d198abbce355).  
Tags: \[Fact\] measurements carried in published material; \[Published model\] models in published papers and their comparison with data; \[Mechanism hypothesis\] mechanisms not tested by intervention; \[Derivation\] proofs of this paper on declared models or under explicitly listed premises; \[Conditional conclusion\] theoretical conclusions with antecedents; \[Prediction\] breachable conditional predictions, not registered as certification; \[Insufficient\] insufficient observation, insufficient representation or a missing theoretical relation; \[Representational choice\] formalization choices of this paper, which do not become mathematical proofs by agreeing with the reading.  
Program marks: T1–T25 denote the sections of the output in Appendix B.2, and T2b, T15e and the like denote items within them. Counterexample marks X18-1–X18-43: see Section 11.  
Clause numbering: D18-1–D18-14, Equations 4.1–4.5, Criteria 4.6(1)–(6), Theorems 18-1–18-10, counterexamples X18-1–X18-43, breaches K18-1–K18-10, open items U18-1–U18-10, predictions PN-1–PN-5, PC-1, PC-2, PP-1, PP-3, PP-4. All are stable numbers, for citation by later papers.

2\. Core Thesis

### 2.1  Central Content

The central content this system must express precisely is this: the transformation of a Trace-Response set is the rewriting of an affected subset, determined jointly by “the value of tendency at the write position” and “the labeled organization of the set”, while tendency is determined in advance by the existing set and its organization. This chain closes over the first six steps of the research procedure for concretized fields (find the Trace-Responses, the single Trace-Response, the organization of the set, historical dynamics forms tendency, tendency carried out as activity, activity acting again on the set); Step 7 calls on the vocabulary and the criteria.  
Five requirements follow:  
　First, Trace-Responses, activity, residues and background are kept in separate layers, and residues enter the running semantics (Theorem 18-1);  
　Second, organization takes the labeled description as its object of computation; relabeling serves only for comparison, and the two kinds of identity, exact and up to relabeling, are not mixed (Theorem 18-2);  
　Third, tendency is defined on the signal class and precedes activity, and activity is the value of tendency at the actual signal; the write reads only its own position and the local activity (Theorem 18-3);  
　Fourth, action and being affected are booked under the declared resolution semantics; duty read-back is split into two branches and aligned with the two readings of the history condition in \[S2\] and \[17\] (Theorem 18-4);  
　Fifth, the bearing of tendency, the rewriting of write laws by history and the change of organizational relations are all represented within the running system, and are not replaced by redeclaration outside the run (Theorems 18-5, 18-8, 18-9).

### 2.2  Upstream Inputs

(1) Ontological source text. *The Ontological Definition of the Sedimentation-Field* v2.0: “A Sedimentation-Field is: the Trace-Response set that signals leave in a medium, together with the mode of organization of this set, transforming under the action of historical dynamics, and its activity.” “Hence Self-Manifestation Theory is the study of the Trace-Responses of signals in a medium and of the transformation of Trace-Response sets. In examining a concretized field, it must first find out what its Trace-Responses are, and then study the transformation of their set under the action of historical dynamics.” Explanatory notes: “A single Trace-Response (element) has only two basic kinds of change, deepening and shallowing; no change is zero change, and the limit of shallowing is that the Trace-Response phenomenon does not occur.” “The Trace-Response set is organized by dynamics, and its transformation rests on the deepening and shallowing of its elements, appearing as dynamic phenomena such as paths, overlap, Edge-Seepage and local shallowing, and as organizational transformations of the whole, of subsets and of their mutual intervention.”  
Delimitation of the Trace-Response (*The Ontological Structure Diagram of the Sedimentation-Field* v0.4.1): the Trace-Response takes the original meaning of Definition 3 of the main book's Mathematical Form of the Field, namely the effective change that Structural Position x sustains and retains at time t; the place of carrying is Structural Position x, and depth corresponds to the Sedimentation-Degree δ(x).  
Reading: “Historical dynamics forms tendency; the carrying-out of tendency is activity; activity and tendency organizing (that is, organizing the set) is transformation, and it transforms according to tendency, as water seeks low ground.” (\[S9\], Section 2.1, F2.) Here “organization” means the mode of organization of the Trace-Response set: connections, paths, overlaps, subsets, mutual constraints and the tendency formed by them.  
*The Ontological Structure Diagram of the Sedimentation-Field* v0.4: the horizontal axis is history H; Tᵢ is the current Trace-Response set; Sᵢ enters Tᵢ from above; Dᵢ is the dynamics produced by the joint action of the signal and the current set, carrying a set of dynamics types; D′ᵢ is dynamic re-action, connected directly to the Trace-Response set Tᵢ₊₁ of the next moment.  
(2) The research procedure for concretized fields (seven steps). It is required content of *The Master Map of the Field Concretization Group Form*: first find the Trace-Responses, then study how the Trace-Response set transforms. Step 1, find the Trace-Responses; Step 2, study the deepening, shallowing and zero change of the single Trace-Response; Step 3, study the organization of the set; Step 4, historical dynamics forms tendency; Step 5, tendency is carried out as activity; Step 6, activity acts again on the set, and the subsequent transformation; Step 7, extract the dynamics types, assemble the evidence, conditional derivations and breachable predictions, and then call on existing criteria to adjudicate. This paper takes charge of the expression, running and solution of Steps 4 to 6, and in 8.3 writes out the formal side of the seven steps link by link.  
(3) Upstream in the Group Form (called only, not redefined).  
\[S1\]: χ = (P, L, B, W); Org(T); Cond; Act; the component criterion; maintenance and rest; HistCond, “its action (occurrence, timing, position, strength or direction) is determined in part, through an actual material route, by Trace-Responses T has already left”; the convergence-round lemma; the in-boundary self-feedback type; items 8 and 9 of the degree of answer.  
\[S2\]: D1, three values; D4–D9, mechanism events, event witnesses, R-layer qualification evaluation, indecomposable joint events (net zero with nonzero action evidence returns ⊥); D10–D21, F-layer states, separable mechanisms (constant, linear and bilinear laws), functional support, available positions, F-layer event atoms, the F-layer recursion 4.2 (only components with Act\_F = 1 are booked), the witness-preserving embedding J; Theorem 1 (cancellation and rest are indistinguishable), Theorem 2; the read-back antecedents of 8.2.  
\[S9\]: grading of tendency directions and share readings; Section 2.1, F2.  
\[S12\]: D, B and F at the path layer; \[S15\]: correspondence between instances; \[S16\]: the vocabulary of dynamics types Φ1–Φ17.  
(4) Inputs from \[17\]. The three-way division (Theorem 17-1, including the robust window of (2)); Lemma 17-1′ (activity that varies with Trace-Responses does not thereby become a Trace-Response); depth order (Theorem 17-2, unique relative to a fixed probe up to strictly increasing transformations); the organization hypergraph (Theorem 17-4′: (1) issues the uniqueness of components only for a given hypergraph, and the physical boundary is not thereby unique; (5) a chain linked across times or across probes that cannot be applied simultaneously must state its frame); the effective-difference certificate (Definition 6′: one sufficient certificate for the strength clause of HistCond of \[S1\], with four clauses: (A) same beat, same process, same target; (B) the contrast changes only Trace-Response positions that have depth \> ε in the pre-beat state of that beat and have evidence of formation and retention earlier than that beat; (C) the total-component difference recomputed under the unclamped mechanism exceeds ε; (D) the route and action certification hold; three-way verdict: effective difference certified, functional support established, effective difference not certified); the normative note (Note 6′-1); the scope of the main verdict and of ⊥\_HC (Definition 7′); same-beat qualification (Theorem 17-5′); cross-record chains (Definition 10′, Theorem 17-8′: nonzero, threshold-crossing difference and actual action are proved separately, under conditions C1, C2, C3′, C4′, C5, C6); 8.2, 8.2.4 (the chain of conditions carried through on the *Drosophila* model), 8.3 (*Physarum polycephalum*). These serve as inputs and are cited as conclusions of \[17\].

### 2.3  Working Input

𝕀₁₈ = (𝔐, σ₀, 𝒮, ε\_L, semantics choice, relabeling subgroup G): the declared host model; the initial complete state (obtained by the identification of \[17\]); the labeled signal class; the resolution; original-mechanism or booked-as-effective semantics; the relabeling subgroup used for comparison (declared as a subgroup, without claiming maximality).  
2.4  Literature (sources of object evidence; the evidential level is noted with each entry, values follow the level noted and have not been recomputed)  
*Drosophila* mushroom body: Hige et al. 2015 (Neuron 88: 985–998); Cohn, Morantte and Ruta 2015 (Cell 163: 1742–1755); Aso and Rubin 2016 (eLife 5: e16135); Felsenberg et al. 2018 (Cell, from secondary summaries); Huang et al. 2024 (Nature 634: 1141–1149, including the accompanying model); Ichinose et al. 2015 (eLife 4: e10719, from secondary summaries); Pavlowsky et al. 2018 (Current Biology 28: 1783–1793, from secondary summaries).  
*Physarum polycephalum*: Kramar and Alim 2021 (PNAS 118: e2007815118); Marbach et al. 2023 (eLife 12: e78100, Equations 1–3 from the published full text); Tero et al. 2010 (Science 327: 439–442); Reid et al. 2012 (PNAS 109: 17490–17494, from the abstract).

3\. Basic Objects and the Sequence of Definitions  
Objects come from three sources: (1) upstream ontological objects, called only: χ, T, Org(T), Fld, TraceSet, Part, HDyn, Cond, Act, HistCond, signal, mechanism process, joint process; (2) existing formal objects, inherited: Structural Position, Trace-Response, Sedimentation-Degree δ, Dτ and Eλ of Equation 2, D4–D21 of \[S2\]; (3) objects newly introduced in this paper, defined one by one below. For every object that involves a relation, its properties are declared item by item in Section 7. Purely formal auxiliary quantities: see 8.4.  
D18-1  Declared host model \[Representational choice\]  
𝔐 = (X̂, b, 𝒮, 𝒴, Φ, P, W, post, τ, ε\_L). X̂: the set of (latent) Structural Positions with identity labels; b: background structure (retained quantities not rewritten by the signal history within the window under discussion); 𝒮: the labeled signal class (including “rest” R); 𝒴: the full activity space; Φ: the read-out law (d, r, b, s) ↦ y; P: the residue law (r, y) ↦ r′; W: the write law, giving a valid process-by-process decomposition W(d, y, s)\_x = d\_x + Σ\_ρ c\_{ρ,x}; post: the declared non-law post-processing (clipping, removal threshold, gridding); τ: time scale; ε\_L: resolution. Post-processing is declared in two kinds: position-wise post-processing (LP)—post(d\_new, d\_old)\_x depends only on (d\_new\_x, d\_old\_x), which covers clipping and position-wise gridding; cross-position post-processing (such as adding two positions, or swapping them) must be declared separately, and no conclusion that depends on LP is issued for it.  
D18-2  Complete mathematical state and source–identity record  
The complete mathematical state σ = (d, r) determines the numerical successor. The source–identity record ℛ (formation record Form, out-of-boundary cause Mark, carrier identity, path certification; inherited from D16 and D13 of \[S2\]) is passed along with the history and is not derivable from σ: two histories with the same σ can have different signal formation, carrier identity and path certification (T13a). Trace-Response membership (D18-3) and qualification read-back (Theorem 18-4) must read both σ and ℛ; the set of formed positions used in read-back contains only positions whose formation has been verified and whose formation beat precedes the current beat—positions formed only at the same beat are not counted, and no arbitrary effective entry substitutes for Form (T23d). The update law of ℛ: at each beat, the event entries (beat, position label, process) of that beat's effective components and pulses are appended to the record. d\_x is the change of the carrier variable w\_x of position x relative to the baseline w⁰\_x, the baseline being subtracted only here and only once (neural side: d = 1 − w/w⁰; flow network: d is conductance, with D⁰ = 0 declared). r is the activity residue. If a latent position x has an existence variable e\_x (D18-12), e\_x is a component of d.  
D18-3  Trace-Response and Trace-Response set  
x ∈ T if and only if \|d\_x\| \> ε\_L, this change is caused by the signal history through the write law (or by in-field continuation, inheriting r of \[S1\]), and it is retained within W (inheriting the three-way division of Theorem 17-1 of \[17\]). Not Trace-Responses: b; y; r (where its time scale is shorter than the window resolution). A law-layer component (D18-11), if written by the signal history and retained, is a Trace-Response under this definition.  
D18-4  Labeled organization description  
O(σ) = (the values of σ on labeled positions, b, {K(s)}, Φ, P, W, post)—the actual object of computation. Derived aspects (no separate state): the recruited subset K(s); the effective subset K\_eff(s; d) (D18-12); overlap; paths; intervention between subsets I(U ← V): whether changing the values of d on V can change the local activity at write positions in U; for its linear-order strength see Theorem 18-5(5).  
D18-5  Constitutive automorphisms and identity up to relabeling  
An element of Aut(𝔐) is g = (π, σ\_g, ψ): π acts on X̂ (and at the same time on the labels of d, r and e, and on the position labels of the entries in ℛ), σ\_g acts on 𝒮 (fixing R), and ψ acts on 𝒴, such that π·b = b, Φ(π·d, π·r, b, σ\_g s) = ψ·Φ(d, r, b, s), P(π·r, ψ·y) = π·P(r, y), W(π·d, ψ·y, σ\_g s) = π·W(d, y, s), and post commutes with π; the append law of ℛ commutes with π (entries record only labels, and relabeling acts entry by entry, T13b). ψ is a permutation (relabeling) of activity coordinates. What is actually used is the declared subgroup G ⊆ Aut(𝔐) (without claiming G = Aut). Identity up to relabeling: σ ≈\_G σ′ ⟺ ∃ g ∈ G, σ′ = π·σ.  
Additional condition under booked-as-effective semantics: the filtered write operator used must commute with g—under booked-as-effective semantics W^ε\_num(π·d, ψ·y, σ\_g s) = π·W^ε\_num(d, y, s), and under the F-layer certified filtered write W^ε\_F(π·d, ψ·y, σ\_g s) = π·W^ε\_F(d, y, s) (for the two operators see D18-8). Commutation of the total write W does not imply commutation of the filtered write (X18-28, T20a). A checkable sufficient condition (process pairing PC\_g): there is a bijection κ\_g on process identities that pairs process ρ at position x with process κ\_g(ρ) at position π(x) such that c\_{κ\_g(ρ),π(x)}(π·d, ψ·y, σ\_g s) = c\_{ρ,x}(d, y, s), with the same threshold ε\_L on both sides—this guarantees that W^ε\_num commutes (T20b, T20c; pairing\_ok in Appendix A.1 takes κ\_g to be the process of the same name); commutation of W^ε\_F further requires that the certification states cert\_eff on the two sides of the pairing be the same (PC\_g^F; cert\_pairing\_ok; when only values and thresholds are preserved but certification is not, W^ε\_num commutes while W^ε\_F does not, T20f). Equivariance of records and of read-back is stated as a separate field transformation: the entries of ℛ (beat, position, process) ↦ (beat, π(position), κ\_g(process)); in Form, Mark, carrier identity and path certification, position labels transform by π and process labels by κ\_g.  
D18-6  Tendency \[Representational choice\]  
Tendency is the inclination to activity that the organization of the set forms under the declared background, residues and input class; concrete activity is its carrying-out under actual signals. Its runnable expression is Φ\_σ := Φ(d, r, b, ·) : 𝒮 → 𝒴, defined on labeled signals. The same Φ can mask different organizations, residue mechanisms or write laws (Theorems 18-1(2), 18-7, 18-8(1)), so organization retains the labeled placement, connections, overlaps and write-back routes (D18-4). “Historical dynamics forms tendency” requires stating which segment of retained change takes part in forming that inclination (indicated by ψ\_x and I(U ← V), Theorem 18-5); defining Φ in equation form does not by itself certify that physical route, and the material reality of the route belongs to the read-back antecedents (8.2). Readings of tendency: ρ(Φ\_σ(s)), where ρ is a partial read-out. Exact sameness of current tendency: σ ≃ σ′ ⟺ Φ\_σ = Φ\_{σ′}; sameness of current tendency up to relabeling: σ ≃\_G σ′ ⟺ ∃ g ∈ G, Φ\_{σ′}(σ\_g s) = ψ·Φ\_σ(s) ∀s.  
D18-7  Activity  
y\_t = Φ\_{σ\_t}(s\_t); r\_{t+1} = P(r\_t, y\_t). Activity-uniqueness antecedent U1: given (σ, b, s), y is unique (under multistability, the slowly varying activity state must be included in r).  
D18-8  Action sets and affected sets; two resolution semantics  
Act⁰ = {x : some c\_{ρ,x} ≠ 0}; Act^ε = {x : some \|c\_{ρ,x}\| \> ε\_L}; Aff⁰ = {x : Δd\_x ≠ 0}; Aff^ε = {x : \|Δd\_x\| \> ε\_L}; the input-induced write set Wr(s) = {x : some activity-mediated component (D18-10) c\_{ρ,x} ≠ 0}.  
(A) Original-mechanism semantics: d′ = post(d + Σ\_ρ c\_ρ); always Aff^ε ⊆ Aff⁰ ⊆ Act⁰ ∪ Post (Post being the positions changed by post-processing); Aff⁰ ⊄ Act^ε and Act^ε ⊄ Aff⁰ (T3).  
(B) Booked-as-effective semantics (the purely numerical filtered operator W^ε\_num): d′ = post(W^ε\_num(d, y, s)), W^ε\_num(d, y, s)\_x := d\_x + Σ\_{ρ : \|c\_{ρ,x}\| \> ε\_L} c\_{ρ,x}; Aff⁰ ⊆ Act^ε ∪ Post; before post, the booked sums of the two semantics differ by Σ\_{0\<\|c\|≤ε} c; after post, actual post-states are compared by their actual values (Theorems 18-4(4), 18-8(2)).  
(C) When ε\_L = 0 the two coincide (T3w).  
(D) The F-layer certified filtered operator W^ε\_F(d, y, s)\_x := d\_x + Σ\_{ρ : \|c\_{ρ,x}\| \> ε\_L ∧ cert\_eff(ρ) = 1} c\_{ρ,x} (4.2 of \[S2\]). It books differently from W^ε\_num on components with \|c\| \> ε\_L but cert\_eff ≠ 1 (T20e). In this paper, “booked-as-effective semantics” and step(sem = "booked") in Appendix A.1 refer to W^ε\_num; on entry into the F layer of \[S2\], W^ε\_F is used (Theorem 18-4(4)).  
D18-9  Local write (LW)  
W(d, y, s)\_x = w\_x(d\_x, y\|N(x), s\|x; c\_x). A direct read of the position’s own value d\_x and of its own direct-read state (such as the law-layer component c\_x) is allowed; these are kept as explicit arguments. LW constrains only the write law W (before booking); locality of the actual post-state additionally requires position-wise post-processing LP (D18-1; X18-29, T21a). Wherever the write passes through an undeclared mediator, the mediator must be listed as activity or residue and given a mechanism correspondence.  
D18-10  The two branch types of components  
Direct-read-of-own-position component: varies with d\_x under fixed local activity (relaxation, own-position terms before clipping); activity-mediated component: varies with local activity under fixed d\_x. A component may have both branches; the two branches are reported separately per Definition 6′ of \[17\]: along the declared order “first swap the activity, then change the direct read”, the sum of the A branch and the D branch is the total-component difference; this is a telescoping identity along that order, not a unique apportionment of responsibility (counterexample X8 of \[17\]).  
D18-11  Law-layer components (consolidation state)  
The coefficients of the write law may depend on another group of retained quantities c: w\_x = w\_x(d\_x, y\|N(x), s; c\_x), and the recursion of c, c′ = C(c, y, s), is part of the write law. When Φ does not read c, c is called a hidden law-layer component.  
D18-12  Derived incidence and existence variables  
Derived incidence: with a declared threshold θ\_e, Inc(d) = {e : d\_e \> θ\_e} (flow network: tube segments with positive conductance carry flow); effective subset K\_eff(s; d) = {x ∈ K(s) : transmission at position x has not reached its limit} (neural: synapses whose efficacy has not been depressed to 0). Existence variables: e\_x ∈ {0, 1} of a latent position x is a component of d; positions with e\_x = 0 carry neither read-out nor write; generation and elimination laws are part of the write components (example: generation along the edge when full).  
D18-13  Tendency received by the position itself, and local loop gain  
Fixing the rest of the complete state σ\_{−x} and the signal s (quasi-static declaration), ψ\_x(δ) := Φ\_{(σ\_{−x}, d\_x = δ)}(s)\|N(x). At differentiable points, the activity-mediated loop gain is L\_x := ∂\_y w\_x · ψ\_x′(δ); the direct-read term ∂\_δ w\_x is counted separately.  
D18-14  Levels of hidden change (exact convention only)  
H0, the null space of the partial reading; H1, the null space of the probes; H2, the null space of tendency (σ ≃ σ′), divided into H2a (manifesting in successors) and H2b (permanently hidden, σ \~ σ′). Exact dynamical identity: σ \~ σ′ ⟺ for every finite history the full activity sequences of the two states agree beat by beat; dynamical identity up to relabeling, \~\_G, is obtained by relabeling per D18-5.

4\. Equations and Criteria  
4.1  Read-out and residue (the carrying-out of tendency)  
　y\_t = Φ(d\_t, r\_t, b, s\_t), r\_{t+1} = P(r\_t, y\_t).  
4.2  Write (activity acting again on the set; D′ᵢ)  
　Original mechanism: d\_{t+1} = post( d\_t + Σ\_ρ c\_ρ(d\_t, y\_t, s\_t) ); booked as effective: d\_{t+1} = post( d\_t + Σ\_{ρ : \|c\_ρ\| \> ε\_L} c\_ρ ).  
　Under LW, c\_{ρ,x} = c\_{ρ,x}(d\_x, y\_t\|N(x), s\_t\|x; c\_x) (law-layer components included).

### 4.3  Law layer and existence

　The law-layer component c and the existence variable e are components of d; their changes are write components (D18-11, D18-12), carried by the same Equation 4.2.  
4.4  The running equation and its correspondence to the diagram  
　Tᵢ (together with rᵢ) ⟶ (read-out law, tendency Φ\_{σᵢ}) ⟶ (Sᵢ arrives) ⟶ activity yᵢ ⟶ (write law: D′ᵢ acts again directly on the set) ⟶ Tᵢ₊₁ ⟶ forms Dᵢ₊₁ jointly with rᵢ₊₁ and Sᵢ₊₁.  
　“Historical dynamics forms tendency”: Φ\_{σᵢ} is borne by the historically written Tᵢ and its organization; “the carrying-out of tendency is activity”: yᵢ = Φ\_{σᵢ}(Sᵢ); “activity and tendency organizing (that is, organizing the set) is transformation”: the arguments of Equation 4.2 are exactly yᵢ and the labeled organization of Tᵢ; “as water seeks low ground”: under LW, the change of each element is determined only by the depth at its own position and the tendency received by the position itself, ψ\_x (Theorems 18-3, 18-5). The set of dynamics types {t₁ … tₙ}: the mechanism components of Equation 4.2 are registered under the vocabulary of \[S16\].  
4.5  Correspondence to Equation 2 of *The Mathematical Form of the Field* (inheriting 6.1 of \[S2\])  
　Components are assigned by mechanism duty: Dτ ↔ deepening components (including activity-mediated deepening writes); Eλ ↔ shallowing-type components (relaxation); law-layer and existence components have no counterpart term in Equation 2 and are registered as R\_other; post-processing (clipping) does not enter Equation 2.  
4.6  Criteria (for the proof of each criterion see the theorem cited)  
　(1) The three conditions for entering the F layer of \[S2\] via J₁₈: an F candidate event table can be generated (including the D20 check on the original certifications; if it fails, the F run is refused and an undetermined R-layer result is reported separately); the numerical trajectory of the original run is reproduced (beat by beat post(d + Σorig)\_x = d\_x + ΣF\_x, with the F-layer state within the D20 domain; on the common domain this reduces to position-wise equality of the booked totals of the two sides); witnesses and qualification are preserved (on the same valid event table, the evaluator of \[S2\] computes the per-event atoms and Trace\_F, Part\_F, HDyn\_F, Q\_F of D19) (Theorem 18-4(4)).  
　(2) Read-back atoms and the complete certificate: numerical support, read certification, path, HistCond\_F, Act\_F, the numeric difference and the item-by-item evidence for the four clauses of Definition 6′ of \[17\] are listed separately; on the frozen declared contrast family, the difference and the certification are computed contrast by contrast, and the complete certificate is the three-valued disjunction over the contrasts of \[\|Δ\| \> ε\_L ∧ four clauses\]; the three-column summary follows the rule in the preamble of Theorem 18-4 (Theorem 18-4).  
　(3) Local stability: \|F′(δ\*)\| \< 1, that is, 0 \< h(γ − L\_x) \< 2 (Theorem 18-5(2)); at set level, ρ(J) \< 1 (Theorem 18-5(5)).  
　(4) Permanent hiding: on the restricted class, C\_{s\_k}B\_{k−1}A\_{k−1}⋯B₀A₀Δ = 0 for every history (B\_j being the linear part of the affine post-processing common to the two arms); on linear time-invariant closed loops, C\_ρA\_cl^kΔ = 0, k = 0, …, n − 1 (Theorem 18-7(1)(4)).  
　(5) Visibility of the law layer: the beat of the first component difference, the common conditions for a difference in actual post-states, the actual taking-effect of setting and weak writes, distinguishability by read-out, and the complete-state condition for overwriting (Theorem 18-8(2)–(5)).  
　(6) Remote effect of removal: under fixed injection ΔQ\_x = Q\_e·T\_x(u→v); under fixed-potential or mixed boundaries ΔQ\_x = Q\_e·T⁰\_x(u→v); each with its solvability antecedents (Theorem 18-9(2)).

5\. Running and Solution  
5.1  Solver (step and run in Appendix A.1): input 𝕀₁₈; execute beat by beat: (1) take the declared domain (the program actually runs only with exact rational arithmetic on finite signal tables; activity uniqueness U1, the valid process-by-process decomposition, LW and LP are guaranteed by declaration and are not verified by step, the corresponding verification being done only in the listed program items); (2) compute y from (d, r, s); (3) compute the components of each process at each position from (d, y, s); (4) book under the declared semantics and apply post-processing; (5) compute r′ from (r, y); (6) record Act⁰, Act^ε, Aff⁰, Aff^ε and the components.

### 5.2  Computable quantities

　Tendency: Φ\_σ is computed signal by signal on a finite signal class (potential).  
　Affected sets and action sets: read out directly at each beat.  
　Exact dynamical identity: finite verification over histories of finite length (exact\_dyn\_same; the resolution ε and the semantics run through both the run and the comparison, original-mechanism semantics by default); universal statements require proof (the induction of Theorem 18-2, or the criterion of Theorem 18-7).  
　Identity up to relabeling: equivariance is verified element by element on the declared subgroup G (original mechanism: T2d; under booking as effective, process pairing is checked in addition, T20c), and orbit membership is compared element by element (T2e, T2h).  
　Permanent hiding: on linear time-invariant closed loops, a Cayley–Hamilton check over k \< n (hidden\_forever\_lti), universal; no general solver is provided for nonlinear, switched or residue-bearing closed loops.  
　Tendency received by the position itself, ψ\_x, and loop gain: fix the rest and sweep the position’s own depth to obtain ψ\_x; compute L\_x at differentiable points (T5, T6).  
　F-layer compilation and the two readings of the history condition: f\_component, f\_supp\_act, histcond\_F, histcond\_F\_full, histcond\_diff, histcond\_diff\_joint (T4, T10); read-back atoms and the three-column summary: readback\_atoms, computed contrast by contrast over the frozen contrast family (T14, T17, T18, T22); the three conditions of J₁₈: j18\_spec compiles the closed-graph conditions declared by the original events, certifications, timing, sources and evidence protocol into the Spec of \[S2\]; j18\_check calls the evaluator field\_final of Appendix A.4 (check decides D20, run computes D19); j18\_raw\_run gives the same run under the original mechanism (values interpreted exactly per exact of \[S2\]); nonneg\_encode gives a nonnegative encoding of signed depth (T23).  
　Steady state, removal and incremental response of general-boundary conductance networks: solve\_bc, flows\_bc, removal\_response (T16).  
5.3  Solvability: Theorem 18-10.

6\. Coupling Relations  
6.1  With \[S1\]: the five layers and the running equations of this paper represent transformation on top of the three duties; for read-back see Section 8 and Theorem 18-4.  
6.2  With \[S2\]: under the three conditions of Theorem 18-4(4), a run of this paper embeds into the F layer via J₁₈ (laws belonging to D12 with post leaving the post-state unchanged are not sufficient by themselves, T10f, T23a); otherwise it enters the R layer as event witnesses: a of ω takes \[\|c\| \> ε\] in model mode with action certification established, and in measurement mode or when certification is not established takes its value by the original certification rule of the R layer of \[S2\] (possibly ⊥); η takes its values by the atoms of Theorem 18-4(2)—HistCond\_F takes the value 0, 1 or ⊥ of the complete conjunction of D18 of \[S2\], and the original results for numerical support, read certification, path and Act are recorded as they are; the numeric difference and the item-by-item evidence for the four clauses of Definition 6′ of \[17\] are recorded as they are; when the complete certificate is 1, that certificate is recorded in addition, and when the numeric difference crosses the threshold but certification is missing or unknown, only the numerical evidence and the unknown are recorded; when Act\_F = 0 for the same event, an external action claim does not change that clause, and the conflict is recorded as it is; independent R-layer witnesses are recorded separately according to their event identity, mode and certification basis, and are not mixed with the F atoms; “effective difference not certified” is only a summary column—0 is not taken on its basis, nor does it override HistCond\_F = 1 where the original antecedents of \[S2\] are met; κ takes the Cond evidence. Indecomposable joint processes follow D9.  
6.3  With \[17\]: the inputs (three-way division of Trace-Responses, depth bearing, organization hypergraph, history-condition witnesses) are taken from the identification of \[17\]; this paper does not re-prove its adjudications and keeps its qualifications when citing them (Theorem 17-4′(1): the uniqueness of components holds for the given hypergraph, and the physical boundary is not thereby unique; Theorem 17-4′(5): a chain linked across times or across probes that cannot be applied simultaneously must state its frame).  
6.4  With *The Mathematical Form of the Field*: Trace-Response ↔ d; Sedimentation-Degree ↔ \|d\|; Equation 2 ↔ the assignment of the components of Equation 4.2 (4.5); the continuation tendency κ is a locally derived quantity, and ψ\_x of this paper explicitly contains the rest of the set beyond the arguments (δ, θ, S) of κ (U18-2).  
6.5  With *The General Form of Sedimentation Dynamics*: Continuation Bias B is “the change in relative travel after the sedimentation difference is smoothed out”, which in this paper is the counterfactual dependence of tendency on differences in d; Theorem 18-7 characterizes the opposite direction (differences that do not manifest).  
6.6  With *The General Form of Self-Manifestation-Field Induction*: the continuation-inclination distribution Πₜ of the tendency structure S is the share reading of Φ\_σ on the declared inputs; the four layers of self-reinforcement (A–D) correspond in this paper respectively to the deepening components of 4.2, the monotonicity of ψ\_x, the order and shares of actual activity, and the generative space (the last is not represented).  
6.7  With \[S9\], \[S12\], \[S15\] and \[S16\]: shares and tendency directions are readings of tendency; D, B and F are at the path layer; transfer between instances follows the correspondence conditions of \[S15\]; dynamics types are registered under the vocabulary of \[S16\], and no new dynamics is claimed.  
6.8  With Marbach et al. 2023: in their Equation 3 the rest of the network enters the shear in the tube itself through R\_net, which corresponds to the form of ψ\_x of this paper under the tube's own pumping drive; their delay-bearing fate criterion and Theorem 18-5 are a structural analogy (Theorem 18-6).

7\. Properties of Relations  
(Clause for Mathematical Forms in the Canon Chapters: reflexivity, symmetry, antisymmetry and transitivity are declared item by item.)  
　≈\_G (identity up to relabeling): reflexive (the identity element belongs to G), symmetric (G is a group; take inverses), transitive (composition); not antisymmetric. If only a subset rather than a subgroup is declared, transitivity can fail, so only subgroups are used.  
　≃, ≃\_G (sameness of current tendency): reflexive, symmetric, transitive; not antisymmetric.  
　\~, \~\_G (dynamical identity): reflexive, symmetric, transitive; not antisymmetric; dependent on the declared signal class 𝒮 (enlarging 𝒮 can separate two identical states, T2c).  
　\= ⇒ \~ ⇒ ≃ ⇒ same activity under the actual signal; ≈\_G ⇒ \~\_G ⇒ ≃\_G; \~ ⇒ \~\_G, ≃ ⇒ ≃\_G: all are implications (inclusions between preorders), and each arrow is strict (Theorem 18-2(2)).  
　I(U ← V) (intervention between subsets): not necessarily reflexive, symmetric or transitive; declared case by case.  
　Act^ε ⊆ Act⁰, Aff^ε ⊆ Aff⁰: set inclusion, a partial order.  
　Derived incidence Inc(d): a function of d, not a relation.  
　J₁₈ (the embedding, Theorem 18-4(4)): a map from a run of this paper to an F run or an R history of \[S2\], not a binary relation on one domain; the four properties do not apply.  
Summary of applicability antecedents: U1, activity uniqueness; LW; LP (locality of the actual post-state); equivariance under booking as effective (W^ε\_num) additionally requires process pairing PC\_g, and equivariance under F-layer certified filtering (W^ε\_F) additionally requires PC\_g^F; the rest quasi-static (for the local conclusions of Theorem 18-5); linear time-invariant closed loops (Theorem 18-7(4)); the three conditions of J₁₈ and the common domain (Theorem 18-4(4)); solvability under fixed injection or the same boundary (Theorem 18-9(2)). Wherever an antecedent fails, the corresponding conclusion is not issued and is handled under the “distortions” entry of the relevant link in 8.3.

8\. Read-Back Rules  
8.1  Read-back to the second component of the ontological definition (term by term)  
　“Trace-Response set” ← T (D18-3); “this set” ← the same T in the labeled organization description (identity across time per \[17\] and Ident of \[S2\]); “mode of organization” ← O(σ), including its inclination layer Φ\_σ (D18-6, \[Representational choice\]); “and its activity” ← y; “transforming under the action of historical dynamics” ← the components of Equation 4.2 whose action T takes part in determining (the two branches of Theorem 18-4); “dynamic re-action” D′ᵢ ← the write law directly gives Tᵢ₊₁.

### 8.2  Read-back to the three duties

Theorem 18-4. The value of the history condition cites Note 6′-1 of \[17\]: “The effective-difference certificate of Definition 6′ proves only one kind of history condition, under the declared control. D15–D18 of Chapter 2 express strength dependence through certified paths and nonzero functional support; failure to obtain an effective-difference certificate cannot be used to negate that dependence, nor does it rule on other modes of participation. The F-layer conclusions of Chapter 2 may be read back under their original antecedents.” The three-way verdict follows Definition 6′ of \[17\]: effective difference certified / functional support established / effective difference not certified. The three columns are evidence summaries; the main verdict combines the actual evidence of each atom (numerical support, read certification, path, Act, numeric difference, item-by-item evidence for the four clauses of Definition 6′) by the original formula (preamble of Theorem 18-4): “effective difference certified” is given only when the four clauses are certified item by item—a numeric difference crossing the threshold does not give it by itself, and an action refutation for the same event (Act\_F = 0) is not overridden by an external claim; “functional support established” is given as the second value of Definition 6′ of \[17\] (some valid contrast gives Δ\_tot ≠ 0 but none exceeds ε, or there is nonzero functional support on a certified path per D15–D18 of \[S2\]), the latter clause being HistCond\_F = 1; “effective difference not certified” is not a negation and does not override a positive determination that meets the original antecedents of \[S2\]. Per Definition 7′ of \[17\], ⊥\_HC is limited to insufficient identification evidence for the current candidate witness: when another qualifying process or an upstream certificate is available, Fld is evaluated by conjunction and disjunction per the original formula of \[S1\] and is not overridden by this candidate's being not certified; when every candidate witness examined stops at the latter two values and there is no upstream certificate, HDyn and Fld are recorded as ⊥; unless there is in addition a complete proof of negation covering the declared readings, 0 is not recorded. The definitions and conclusions of \[S2\] are unchanged.  
Read-back antecedents for a reading of 1: the antecedents of 8.2 of \[S2\] (formation and retention of Trace-Responses, identity across time, individuation of mechanisms and carrier attribution, material reality of routes) are borne by the identification and certification of \[17\], and are not produced by runs of this system.  
8.3  Read-back to the research procedure for concretized fields (the seven steps link by link: what bears each, how it connects, conditions of validity, distortions of expression)  
Link 1  Find the Trace-Responses  
Borne by: the retained change d relative to the baseline on Structural Positions (D18-2, D18-3), borne by the hosting mechanism (identification given by the three-way division of Theorem 17-1 and the depth bearing of Theorem 17-2 of \[17\]); activity y, residue r and background b are listed separately.  
Connection: T is an argument of the read-out law and enters Link 4 (tendency).  
Conditions of validity: declared time scale and window; the robust window of the three-way division (Theorem 17-1(2) of \[17\]); identity across time.  
Distortions: treating activity as Trace-Response (activity that varies with Trace-Responses does not thereby become a Trace-Response, Lemma 17-1′ of \[17\]); omitting residues (Theorem 18-1(4)); subtracting the baseline twice; treating the existence of a carrier as a Trace-Response.  
Link 2  The single Trace-Response: deepening, shallowing and zero change  
Borne by: the components at position x in Equation 4.2: positive components deepen and negative components shallow; outside Aff there is zero change; cancellation is maintenance under the action of dynamics (maintenance and rest of \[S1\]); the limit of shallowing is d\_x reaching its limiting value (synaptic efficacy returns to baseline; a tube segment with conductance 0 vanishes, D18-12). Which quantity bears depth is adjudicated by Theorem 17-2 of \[17\] (unique relative to a fixed probe up to strictly increasing transformations).  
Connection: the components at position x are determined by the local activity of Link 5 and the depth at its own position (Theorem 18-3); their sum returns to the set through Link 6.  
Conditions of validity: a valid process-by-process decomposition; declared resolution semantics (D18-8).  
Distortions: reporting only the net change and dropping components (cancellation and rest are indistinguishable, Theorem 1 of \[S2\]); subthreshold components going different ways under the two semantics without this being declared (Theorem 18-4(4)); the write law itself being rewritten by history without being represented (Theorem 18-8); crossing the baseline into a Trace-Response of another type while still counting it as the same type (potentiation of a depression-type Trace-Response is shallowing; crossing the baseline requires declaring another type).  
Link 3  The organization of the set  
Borne by: the labeled organization description O(σ) (D18-4): placement, recruited subsets and overlap, paths, compartments and convergent wiring, read-out law and write law; effective subsets and derived incidence are derived from d (D18-12); the boundary and components of the organization are given by Theorem 17-4′ of \[17\] (which issues the uniqueness of components only for a given hypergraph).  
Connection: O(σ) determines tendency in advance (Link 4); the write of Link 6 can change O(σ), including derived incidence and existence (Theorem 18-9).  
Conditions of validity: the declared model and relabeling subgroup; in comparison, the exact and relabeling conventions are not mixed (Theorem 18-2).  
Distortions: shrinking to an unlabeled list of numbers (Theorem 18-1(2)); reporting only the connection graph (Theorem 18-2(3)); using relabeling classes as the object of computation (Theorem 18-1(3)); treating hypergraph components as physical boundaries (the restriction of Theorem 17-4′(1) of \[17\]); changing the organization by redeclaration outside the run (Theorem 18-9(4)).  
Link 4  Historical dynamics forms tendency  
Borne by: tendency Φ\_σ (D18-6) is borne by T and its organization through the read-out law; for position x, the action of the rest of the set is borne by a single curve, the tendency received by the position itself, ψ\_x (Theorem 18-5(1)); under linear convergence it passes through the intercept, and in conductance networks through (V\_x, R\_x) or R\_net (Theorems 18-5(4), 18-6).  
Connection: the value of tendency at the actual signal is the activity of Link 5; the product of the slope of ψ\_x and the write response is the loop gain, which decides whether the rewrite of Link 6 is self-reinforcing or self-limiting (Theorem 18-5(2)(3)).  
Conditions of validity: U1; LW; the local conclusions require the rest to be quasi-static and the structural branch to be fixed, and nothing is issued where \|F′\| = 1; otherwise set-level linearization is used on the complete state (Theorem 18-5(5), restricted to differentiable and feasible neighborhoods).  
Distortions: treating tendency as an alias of activity that has already occurred (Theorem 18-1(2), X18-2: same actual activity but different tendency); shrinking tendency to a local quantity at its own position (the local form of κ in *The Mathematical Form of the Field* does not explicitly contain the rest of the set, Theorem 18-5(4)); treating partial readings (behavior, boundary pressure difference) as full activity (Theorem 18-7(4)); comparing outside the layer that bears tendency (on a depression-type Trace-Response, the MBON drive of its own channel decreases monotonically with the depth at its own position, so comparison must be declared at the layer of DAN recruitment and inter-compartment balance); substituting Φ defined in equation form for certification of the physical route (D18-6).  
Link 5  Tendency carried out as activity  
Borne by: the read-out law y = Φ\_σ(s), in which the external signal s and the internal continuation r both take part (D18-7); recruitment, passage, activation or inactivation fall on components of y (MBON and DAN firing; tube-segment flow and pressure).  
Connection: the local value of y at write positions enters Link 6 (Theorem 18-3(3)); y enters the next beat through the residue law.  
Conditions of validity: U1; where there is a residue, the complete state includes r.  
Distortions: multistable activity without listing the slowly varying activity as r; reporting only a partial read-out; treating a probe that reads tendency as non-invasive (the probe is a write, Theorem 18-3(4), T1g; non-invasiveness is defined by the actual post-state d′ = d, r′ = r).  
Link 6  Activity acts again on the set  
Borne by: the components of the write law (D′ᵢ acts directly on Tᵢ₊₁); action sets and affected sets are booked under the declared semantics (D18-8); the rewriting of law-layer components and existence variables is also a component (Theorems 18-8, 18-9); duty read-back is split into the direct-read-of-own-position branch and the activity-mediated branch (Theorem 18-4, Theorem 17-5′ of \[17\]).  
Connection: the rewritten T and organization determine the next tendency (back to Link 4), and the chain closes (Equation 4.4).  
Conditions of validity: LW; declaration of the reading for read-back (Theorem 18-4(2)); on entering the F layer, Theorem 18-4(4) is met; a chain on a real object lies on the same declared set, compatible mechanisms and the same time course (the joining of different records follows antecedents C1–C6 of Theorem 17-8′ of \[17\]).  
Distortions: substituting “Φ varies with d” for the process-by-process history condition (tendency varying with the set does not give the history condition of any one write process; status note to Theorem 18-3); projecting a full-activity criterion onto partial readings (Theorem 18-7(4)); splicing mutually incompatible records directly into a closed chain; leaving changes of law-layer components or of organizational relations outside the run.  
Link 7  Extract the dynamics types, then verify and adjudicate  
Borne by: the mechanism components of Equation 4.2 are registered under the vocabulary of \[S16\]; verification by criteria calls the routing of \[S3\], \[S11\], \[S13\] and \[17\].  
Conditions of validity and distortions: registration in the vocabulary does not provide transfer of theorems (5.1 of \[S16\]); model conclusions are kept apart from certification in reality; ⊥ does not imply 0.  
8.4  Purely formal auxiliary quantities (no counterpart on the structural side)  
Elements of the relabeling subgroup G and the process pairing κ\_g; the powers in the Cayley–Hamilton check; the contrast identities of a contrast family; the upper bound on history length in finite exhaustion; the gridding step (a declared rule of the flow-network model); J₁₈; the zero-increment boundary at fixed-potential nodes in the incremental response T⁰; default values of the explicit evidence interface.

9\. Correspondence Tables

### 9.1  Master table

　Trace-Response set \| T (D18-3) \| efficacy depression of KC × compartment synapses \| change in tube-segment conductance relative to before stimulation  
　Element deepening, shallowing, zero change \| components of Equation 4.2, Aff \| DAN-gated depression write, natural recovery, inactive positions not written \| expansion, contraction, disappearance  
　Mode of organization \| O(σ) (D18-4) \| placement, K(s) and overlap, inter-compartment return and convergence, per-compartment rules \| network graph, loops and cut edges, pumping distribution  
　Tendency \| Φ\_σ, ψ\_x (D18-6, D18-13) \| PPL1-α3 = −a + bΣ\_{K(s)} d\_γ1; intercept of MBON drive \| Equation 3: 1/(1 + R/R\_net) and pressure  
　Activity \| y (D18-7) \| KC, MBON and DAN firing; behavioral approach and avoidance \| cycle-averaged flow, pressure, shear  
　Auxiliary memory \| r (D18-2) \| KC adaptation \| sensed shear τ\_s

9.2  Seven-step run for the *Drosophila* mushroom body KC→MBON (declared T = the synapses on the CS+ KC subset in γ1pedc ∪ α3; the chain of Felsenberg et al. is declared separately with T′ = γ1pedc ∪ γ5 and is not spliced with it)  
Step 1  Find the Trace-Responses  
\[Fact\] Signal: odors activate sparse KC subsets; shock or sugar gives a teaching signal through compartment-specific PPL1 and PAM dopaminergic neurons (Hige et al. 2015; Huang et al. 2024). Change left: after pairing, MBON-γ1pedc firing to CS+ goes 118 ± 8.3 → 24 ± 7.4, CS+ charge transfer under voltage clamp drops by 90% ± 3.7%, and KC excitability is unchanged (Hige et al. 2015)—the change is in synaptic transmission; the CS+ depression of MBON-α3 lasts more than 24 h (Huang et al. 2024, at the firing layer). \[Mechanism hypothesis\] The change in α3 lies at the KC→MBON-α3 synapses (an assumption of the accompanying model of Huang et al., not measured directly). Retention: γ1pedc at least 40 min (Hige et al.) and less than 1 h (Huang et al.), the two papers' time courses being in tension; α3 more than 24 h.  
\[Derivation\] Trace-Response d\_{ic} = 1 − w\_{ic}/w⁰\_{ic}; the carrier is the synapse, and the Trace-Response is the change in efficacy; activity is KC, MBON and DAN firing; residue includes KC odor adaptation (the KC adaptation term of the accompanying model of Huang et al.); background is the connectivity and the MBON→DAN wiring. For the basis of identification see 8.2(1) of \[17\].  
\[Insufficient: insufficient observation\] The α3 synaptic layer has not been measured directly; receptors, presynaptic release and structural change have not been resolved.  
Step 2  The single Trace-Response  
\[Fact\] Deepening: depression forms when KCs and DANs are activated in pairing, and reverse pairing is ineffective (Hige et al. 2015). Shallowing: γ1pedc recovers naturally; unpaired DAN activation potentiates γ4 MBON responses (Cohn, Morantte and Ruta 2015). Zero change: synapses of KCs that are not activated are not written, compartment-specifically (Hige et al. 2015). Saturation: depression of 80%–90%, near bottom.  
\[Published model\] The accompanying model of Huang et al. 2024: a signed linear write (depression when DAN drive is positive, potentiation when it is negative), with the decay time constant of the α module switching 3 h after the last training—a clock-controlled rewriting of the write law.  
\[Derivation\] In this representation, potentiation of a depression-type Trace-Response is shallowing; crossing the baseline makes it a potentiation-type Trace-Response, which must be declared as another type. The historical rewriting of the write law is represented by law-layer components (Theorem 18-8): the 3 h switch of Huang et al. is the clock-controlled form; for the manifestation conditions of the history-controlled form and the discriminating experiments, see Theorem 18-8(2)(3)(6) and PC-1, PC-2.  
Step 3  The organization of the set  
\[Fact\] The KC subsets of different odors overlap, and the degree of depression varies with KC overlap with CS+ (Hige et al. 2015, r = 0.90); the same KC axon passes through several compartments, each compartment with its own rules (Hige et al. 2015); MBON-γ1pedc is an inhibitory input to PPL1-α3, and blocking its transmission abolishes the training-dependent response of PPL1-α3 (Huang et al. 2024).  
\[Derivation\] Organization description = placement of the Trace-Responses on labeled KC × compartment positions + recruited subsets K(s) and overlap + inter-compartment return and convergence + the per-compartment rules of write, shallowing and (if any) consolidation. The intervention between subsets I(α3 ← γ1) ≠ 0, with linear-order strength ηb (Theorem 18-5(5): the partial derivative of the α3-position components with respect to γ1-position depth). Effective overlap is derived from depth: if the depression at shared KC positions reaches its limit, the tendency at the overlap probe changes (Theorem 18-9(1), T9a, T9b). Isomorphism of connection graphs does not suffice for identity of organization (Theorem 18-2(3), T2i).  
Step 4  Historical dynamics forms tendency  
\[Fact\] The response of PPL1-α3 to CS+ turns from a decrease to an increase during training and returns to a decrease after 1 h; same odor, different training histories, responses of opposite sign, depending on MBON-γ1pedc transmission (Huang et al. 2024). The response of PPL1-γ1pedc to CS+ is decreased throughout (Huang et al. 2024).  
\[Derivation\] The tendency received at the α3 write positions: ψ(γ1 subset) = PPL1-α3(s) = −a + bΣ\_{K(s)} d\_γ1 (the FLY model of Appendix A.2), borne by the depression distribution of the γ1 subset through MBON-γ1pedc→PPL1-α3; the depth of the α3 positions themselves does not enter ψ (the activity-mediated loop gain at the α3 positions is 0, and the fate of α3 is determined by the tendency of another compartment; T19d). The self-loop of the γ1 compartment: depression lowers the MBON-γ1pedc drive; if MBON-γ1pedc→PPL1-γ1pedc is inhibitory and the disinhibition suffices to bring PPL1-γ1pedc across threshold, it is a positive loop with continued writing, otherwise there is no continued writing (Theorem 18-5(3), T5c, T5d); PPL1-γ1pedc remains decreased after training, suggesting that γ1 lies in the “insufficient disinhibition” regime (a conditional reading). On a depression-type Trace-Response, the MBON drive of its own channel decreases monotonically with the depth at its own position, so comparison must be declared at the layer that bears tendency (DAN recruitment, inter-compartment balance).  
Step 5  Tendency carried out as activity  
\[Fact\] KC, MBON and DAN firing and behavioral approach and avoidance at actual presentation (Huang et al. 2024).  
\[Derivation\] y = Φ\_σ(s); KC adaptation is the residue r; reading tendency is writing (Theorem 18-3(4), T1g): the “strengthening” seen when α3 is tested early with CS+ includes the probe's write.  
Step 6  Activity acts again on the set  
A chain made of mutually compatible experiments within one paper (Huang et al. 2024; the same training protocol and time course; imaging and blockade of different cell types used different groups of animals, so the whole chain was not measured simultaneously in the same animal):  
(1) Training: CS+ paired with shock → PPL1-γ1pedc and PPL1-α3 activity → \[Mechanism hypothesis\] short-term depression written at K(CS+)×{γ1pedc}, α3 written (in the accompanying model, shock drives PPL1-α3 directly).  
(2) Unpaired CS+ 10 min after training: \[Fact\] PPL1-α3 firing rises (depending on MBON-γ1pedc transmission), and the plasticity reading at the MBON-α3 layer strengthens; \[Mechanism hypothesis\] the affected subset is only K(CS+)×{α3}, and the depression deepens.  
(3) Unpaired CS+ 2 h after training: \[Fact\] the plasticity reading at the MBON-α3 layer weakens; \[Mechanism hypothesis\] the short γ1 trace has shallowed, PPL1-α3 returns to inhibition, and the signed write makes the α3 depression shallow.  
(4) The rewritten set forms the next tendency: where α3 depression is deep, MBON-α3 drive is lower at the next CS+.  
\[Derivation\] In the FLY model, (1)–(4) close within the running system; the α3 write is an activity-mediated component and can be compiled into a linear law of \[S2\] (without clipping, T4e), and it enters the R layer with clipping (T4f); the re-exposure reversal latency n*(D) is a step function of the initial γ1 depth, n*(D) = n ⟺ a/(2b r1^(n−1)) \< D ≤ a/(2b r1ⁿ) (T19a, T19b). Theorem 17-8′ of \[17\], on its declared model, joins record one (voltage imaging plus blockade) and record two (graded optogenetic drive of PPL1-α3): at a delay of 2 beats, effective difference is certified and actual action is certified (condition C6), and together with same-beat, same-process and contrast-position qualification this gives a conditional witness of HDyn (8.2.4 of \[17\]).  
Another chain (Felsenberg et al. 2018): the original γ1pedc depression is retained, and extinction sets up, in the γ5 segment, a separate depression written by PAM-γ5, converging on MBON-γ5β′2a—a parallel opposing transformation, declared separately with T′ (Theorem 18-7(5)).  
Step 7  Dynamics types and verification  
Dynamics types (vocabulary of \[S16\]): Φ1b third-factor variant ⊗g DAN gating (Φ9 type); Φ5a threshold read-out; Φ2 shallowing; Φ3a clipping; γ1pedc→α3 is Φ16 of the disinhibition-gate type (cross-subset gating); the Felsenberg type is Φ16 parallel opposing ∥; consolidation, if history-controlled, is registered additionally as R\_other (law-layer write). Shared mechanisms: use-dependent write, shallowing, saturation, tendency-mediated write. Host-specific: third-factor channels and compartmentalization (transmission activity separated from the write signal), signed write, the read-out direction of the depression type, parallel opposing layers, possibly history-controlled consolidation.  
Competing explanations: clock-controlled consolidation (the 3 h switch of the accompanying model); the change in PPL1-α3 passing partly through pathways other than MBON-γ1pedc; chronic TnT blockade changing the basal state; KC adaptation bearing part of the short-term effect.  
\[Fact\] Blocking MBON-α1 for 1 h immediately after training impairs long-term memory (Ichinose et al. 2015), and blocking MVP2 0.5–1 h after training impairs long-term memory (Pavlowsky et al. 2018)—suggesting that consolidation depends on post-training circuit activity, compatible with the history-controlled reading; compartments were not distinguished.  
Breachable predictions:  
　PN-1: when the γ1pedc depression depths left by two training groups (measured by the decrease in the MBON-γ1pedc response) fall, with error taken into account, in different first-arrival intervals, the delays at which early unpaired CS+ switches from deepening to shallowing α3 differ and are ordered by interval (model example: midpoints of the n = 3 and n = 9 intervals; within error δ = 5/216 the first-arrival beat is preserved, T19c); different depths within the same interval with the same delay do not constitute a breach (T19a); only results that this fixed-parameter model excludes under its antecedents are excluded.  
　PN-2: if MBON-γ1pedc is activated optogenetically during re-exposure (canceling the read-out of the γ1 Trace-Response), re-exposure at any delay no longer deepens α3; the explanation via pathways other than MBON-γ1pedc predicts that some deepening remains. \[Insufficient\] Huang et al. performed the activation only during training.  
　PN-3: after late re-exposure weakens α3, if α3 depression recovers after long rest, the single-variable same-synapse reading is refuted; non-recovery alone cannot decide the matter (the equal-rate parallel reading does not recover either, Theorem 18-7(5)).  
　PN-4: if PPL1-α3 output is blocked during measurement, the early “strengthening” disappears while the MBON-γ1pedc depression reading is unchanged (the probe is a write).  
　PN-5: when two training histories give the same total γ1 depression on the CS+ subset but different distributions, the MBON-γ1pedc response is the same at CS+ and different at partially overlapping probe odors (Theorems 18-2(2), 18-9, T2b, T2c).  
　PC-1 (conditional on all four items of Theorem 18-8(5) holding, including the antecedent on differences in actual post-states: post-processing is the identity on the target component, or the comparison is made on values after the actual post-processing; and inheriting the residue antecedent of Theorem 18-8: the residue states (such as KC adaptation) of the two groups before weak training are the same and residues are not driven directly by the consolidation state, otherwise the actual readings of the two groups are compared): a previously set consolidation state is predicted to show up in subsequent weak training—weak training actually writes α3 (MBON-α3 depression measurable) without crossing the consolidation threshold, the animals then rest and are read out by the MBON-α3 response to CS+ (the read-out not on a plateau), and the readings of the two groups are predicted to differ. Conditions carried: the read-out itself is a write (the probe is a write, Theorem 18-3(4)), so the read-out probe must be the same for both groups and its write must be counted; the direct write of weak training must be the same in both groups; the two groups are divided by declared training history, not grouped after the fact by read-out results. This is a prediction under these conditions, not “shows up only in weak training”: when the existing α3 depth is nonzero, the consolidation-state difference could already show up through rest (T8b); strong training no longer produces a difference afterwards when the complete states after overwriting are the same (Theorem 18-8(4)), but cannot erase state differences already produced before (T15f).  
　PC-2 (antecedents as in Theorem 18-8(6), the blockade antecedents being carried together with the prediction): the comparison is between two groups under the same training and the same read-out, “blocking PPL1-α3 output within the post-training consolidation window” and “sham blockade”; a further control with blockade only and no training separates side effects of the blockade on direct write, residues or other pathways; the read-out probe is the same for both groups. The predictions under each of the two mechanism hypotheses: history-controlled reading—the consolidation write depends on activity within the window, and the 24 h α3 depression of the blockade group is weaker than that of the sham group; clock-controlled reading (the 3 h switch of the time constant after the last training in the accompanying model of Huang et al.)—the consolidation part does not depend on activity within the window, and the difference between the blockade and sham groups comes only from direct write and side effects already calibrated by the control group. If the blockade also changes the direct write or residues and the control group cannot separate them, PC-2 discriminates only between the complete premise sets and is not localized on its own to the consolidation mechanism.  
Verification: qualification verdicts follow 8.2(3) of \[17\]; no new verdicts are issued.  
9.3  Seven-step run for the tube network of *Physarum polycephalum*  
Step 1  Find the Trace-Responses  
\[Fact\] Signal: a local nutrient stimulus beside the network (a heat-killed bacterial pellet, or a glucose-plus-leucine solution) (Kramar and Alim 2021). Change left: a new distribution of tube diameters forms within about 15 min and persists to 45 min; nearby tubes enlarge (up to about twofold) and thinner distant tubes shrink; after the nutrient is exhausted, a ring of thick tubes around it remains (a single sequence, not quantified) (Kramar and Alim 2021).  
\[Derivation\] The Trace-Response is the change in tube-segment conductance (∝ r⁴/ℓ) relative to before stimulation; the carrier is the tube segment; activity is the back-and-forth shuttle streaming, pressure and shear; residue is the delay variable of sensed shear (τ\_s in the model of Marbach et al., t\_delay about 1–3 min); the extracellular slime trail (Reid et al. 2012) is a Trace-Response on another medium, and including it requires declaring another object domain. \[Insufficient: insufficient observation\] In vivo the baseline drifts with the contraction cycle and must be declared as a cycle average.  
Step 2  The single Trace-Response  
\[Fact\] Deepening is expansion, shallowing is contraction, and the limit of shallowing is disappearance of the tube segment (Marbach et al. 2023: in a close-up, 4 of n = 12 contract and vanish; pruning is sequential and comes in avalanches); radii range over 5–70 µm; growth fronts generate new tube segments (Tero et al. 2010).  
\[Published model\] Marbach et al. 2023, Equations 1 and 2: d⟨a⟩/dt = (⟨a⟩/t\_adapt)·(τ\_s²/τ₀² − 1), dτ\_s/dt = −(τ\_s − ⟨τ⟩)/t\_delay. ⟨a⟩ is the tube radius averaged over the peristaltic contraction cycle (about 1–2 min); t\_adapt is the time scale of tube-wall growth or disassembly (fitted in that paper at about 10–100 min); τ\_s is the shear rate sensed by the tube wall, lagging behind the cycle-averaged shear rate ⟨τ⟩ (with the dimension of inverse time); τ₀ is the reference shear rate (corresponding to the Murray steady state, varying with local pressure); t\_delay is the sensing delay (about 2 min). The right-hand sides of both equations have the dimension of the corresponding variable divided by time.  
\[Derivation\] In this representation the element-level write has both a direct read of its own position (the ⟨a⟩ factor) and activity mediation (τ\_s via shear); the limit of shallowing is accompanied by a change of derived incidence (Theorem 18-9(1)(2)).  
Step 3  The organization of the set  
\[Fact\] The network has loops and dendritic dangling ends; pruning shrinks first from the loop center (Marbach et al. 2023); there are two dynamic types, near to and far from the stimulus (Kramar and Alim 2021).  
\[Derivation\] Organization description = placement of conductances on the network graph + loops and cut edges + pumping distribution; Kirchhoff conservation makes intervention between subsets globally nonzero (J of Theorem 18-5(5) is in general not sparse); the same multiset of conductances placed differently gives different flow allocations (T1c).  
Step 4  Historical dynamics forms tendency  
\[Published model\] Marbach et al. 2023, Equation 3: the cycle-averaged shear rate of the tube itself is ⟨τ⟩ ≃ 4Q\_in(⟨a⟩)/(π⟨a⟩³) · 1/(1 + R(⟨a⟩)/R\_net); Q\_in is the cycle-averaged net flow rate produced by the tube's own contraction (∝ ⟨a⟩²), R = 8μL/(π⟨a⟩⁴) is the tube's hydraulic resistance, and R\_net is the equivalent resistance that the rest of the network presents to the tube (obtained from Kirchhoff's laws); pressure enters through τ₀. The paper's cues to fate fall into three layers: resistance regimes (R \< R\_net expected stable, R \> R\_net expected to shrink; tubes with R/R\_net \> 1 vanish, while nearly parallel neighboring tubes with R/R\_net ≃ 1 remain stable); the fixed points of the given model (two stable fixed points, zero shear and the Murray steady state, with one unstable fixed point between them); and the empirical parameters of network integration (relative resistance R/R\_net and local pressure). At a dangling end R\_net is undefined, and fate is determined by local pressure.  
\[Derivation\] This is the published form of the tendency received by the tube itself, ψ\_x (Theorem 18-6); the rest of the network bears on it through R\_net (and pressure); the counterpart in this paper under a fixed-injection external source is the Thevenin form, and the organizational quantity is likewise R\_net (d\_xR\_x = R\_net/R).  
Step 5  Tendency carried out as activity  
\[Fact\] Periodic contraction of 1–2 min, with shuttle flow (Marbach et al. 2023). \[Derivation\] Activity y = the cycle-averaged flow rate and pressure of each tube; the drive is distributed peristaltic pumping (in that paper Q\_in ∝ ⟨a⟩²); the residue τ\_s lags behind shear (in that paper: changes in tube diameter lag behind changes in shear rate, by about 2 min on average).  
Step 6  Activity acts again on the set  
\[Fact\] A chain within one and the same paper, Kramar and Alim 2021: stimulus → (\[Mechanism hypothesis\] a softening agent is advected along the flow path; the authors state that the dilation wave speed, about 15 µm/s, is comparable to the particle advection speed; not tested by intervention) → dilation nearby, contraction followed by dilation farther away → a new tube-diameter hierarchy is retained → the response to later stimuli is organized by the existing thick tubes (existing thick tubes near the stimulus hardly contract and then recover; those farther away contract as a whole). \[Insufficient\] No unstimulated or sham-stimulation control.  
\[Published model\] Marbach et al. 2023: the vanishing of one tube merges the neighboring tubes into a longer tube, whose R and R/R\_net rise so that it loses stability, and tubes vanish in succession (avalanche).  
\[Derivation\] In this representation: the affected set is determined by the values of ψ\_x at each tube (Theorem 18-3); the avalanche is a topological change in tendency caused by a change in derived incidence (Theorem 18-9(1)(2), T9c); the loop-gain criterion (Theorem 18-5(2), a condition at the fixed point) and the resistance regimes of Marbach et al. stand in a structural analogy (Theorem 18-6(2)); the one is neither a direct instance of the other nor an interchangeable fate criterion; the witness T5 under the Tero-type law (same conductance, same shear, yet one survives and one perishes) is a correspondence of the same type, not a new explanation.  
Step 7  Dynamics types and verification  
Dynamics types: Φ1a use-dependent deepening; Φ2 shallowing; Φ15 flow division and share-based writes; Φ13 structural events (vanishing of tube segments); Φ17 generation of Structural Positions. Host-specific features: the flow itself is both activity and write drive; global coupling through the conservation constraint; time-lagged residue; advection-mediated writes (mechanism hypothesis).  
Competing explanations and comparison baseline: the published model of Marbach et al. (time-lagged shear feedback + R\_net + pressure) is the baseline, and the representation of 9.3 does not stand opposed to it; the softening-agent advection of Kramar and Alim (mechanism hypothesis); growth fronts and the physiology of dangling ends.  
Breachable predictions:  
　PP-1: discriminating the drive laws—on the reconstructed network, integrate the fate of each tube separately under the two drives (the tube's own peristaltic pumping, Marbach et al. 2023 Equations 1–3; a near-fixed-injection external source, the Tero-type law of this paper), each with its own boundary conditions (fixed potential or fixed injection, Theorem 18-9(2)) and its own dynamic equations, stating the initial state (⟨a⟩, R\_net and pressure of each tube, measured before stimulation), the time window (e.g. 0–60 min after stimulation) and the event threshold (a tube is recorded as vanished once its radius falls below the declared lower limit); compare the fates from each computation with the measured fates. R/R\_net \< 1 and the instantaneous initial-state L\_x \< γ are not taken as two directly comparable sufficient fate criteria (Theorem 18-6(2)); a significant partial correlation is not taken as grounds for exclusion.
