　PP-3: after laser severing of one tube, the direction and ranking of the changes in fate of non-adjacent tube segments can be predicted by recomputing R\_net before and after severing (a direct test of the avalanche mechanism of Marbach et al.); the recomputation must use the boundary conditions under which the experiment actually runs (fixed injection or fixed potential, Theorem 18-9(2)). \[Insufficient\] The two cited papers contain no ablation intervention.  
　PP-4: on tube segments that carry flow there is no tendency null space; moving the stimulus site so that a tube segment without flow comes to carry flow reveals its conductance difference; otherwise a tube segment without flow only converges under the shallowing law and eventually vanishes (T1d).  
Verification: \[S3\] 7.5, Instance B judged ⊥; the identification results of \[17\] 8.3; no new verdicts are issued.

10\. Theorems  
Theorem 18-1 (layers and functionality)  
(1) O(σ) ↦ Φ\_σ is a function; under U1, (σ, s) ↦ y is a function.  
(2) In σ ↦ O(σ) ↦ Φ\_σ ↦ y, the last two steps are in general not injective. O ↦ Φ: different labeled organization descriptions with the same tendency—same d, different c, and Φ does not read c (T25a, i.e. the two states of T8a). With the actual signal fixed, Φ ↦ y: different tendencies with the same activity (T1d, T25b). Further: the multiset of element values does not determine organization—with the same multiset and a different placement, the tendency differs and so does the subsequent transformation (T1a, T1c, T2a); with the same σ and different signals, the activity differs while the Trace-Responses are unchanged (T1b); this is the value of the tendency at different signals and bears neither of the two items above.  
(3) The class of identity up to relabeling does not determine the tendency at fixed labels (T2e: two states on the same orbit give MBON-γ1 at A as 1 versus 2).  
(4) If the residue is omitted and the tendency is written with d as its only argument, then two complete states with the same d and different r give different activity under the same input, and the tendency is undefined: a distortion (T1e). When r can be eliminated uniquely (example: the invariant domain {r = 0, d + s \< 1}, T1f), it reduces to d as the only argument.  
Proof: (1) From D18-4, D18-6 and U1. (2), (3), (4) From the listed constructions. ∎  
Status: definitional unfolding plus witnesses \[Lemma\]. It bears two requirements: tendency must not be merely an alias for the results of activity ((2), second item); organization must not shrink to an unlabeled list of numbers ((2), third item; (3)).  
Theorem 18-2 (relabeling equivariance and the layering of the two identities)  
(1) Equivariance: if g ∈ G ⊆ Aut(𝔐) and σ′ = π·σ, then under the original-mechanism semantics, for every h, the activity, Trace-Responses and residue along σ\_g h starting from σ′ are, beat by beat, ψ and π applied to those along h starting from σ. Under the booked-as-effective semantics (W^ε\_num), the same conclusion additionally requires the W^ε\_num commutation of D18-5 (a sufficient condition is process pairing PC\_g); a run with the F-layer certified filtered write (W^ε\_F) further requires W^ε\_F commutation (sufficient condition PC\_g^F, i.e. PC\_g plus equal certification status on the two paired sides). For a g that satisfies only total-write commutation, equivariance under booking as effective is not issued (X18-28); for a g that satisfies only PC\_g, equivariance under W^ε\_F is not issued (T20f). ℛ corresponds entry by entry according to its append law and the field transformations listed in D18-5. Universal equivariance applies only to the numerical quantities that satisfy the commutation conditions of D18-5 and to records whose commutation has been proved; for the others it is not issued.  
(2) Relabeling convention: σ ≈\_G σ′ ⇒ σ \~\_G σ′ ⇒ σ ≃\_G σ′; exact convention: σ = σ′ ⇒ σ \~ σ′ ⇒ σ ≃ σ′ ⇒ the same activity under the actual signal; cross-convention: \~ ⇒ \~\_G, ≃ ⇒ ≃\_G. Strictness witnesses for each arrow are given in the proof.  
(3) Isomorphism of connection graphs does not suffice to give identity up to relabeling: the γ1↔α3 compartment swap preserves the KC × compartment connection graph but is not in Aut (with ψ taken as a permutation of activity coordinates)—what fails is the read-out commutation identity.  
Proof: (1) Induction on the number of beats. Original mechanism: y′\_t = Φ(π·d\_t, π·r\_t, b, σ\_g s\_t) = ψ·y\_t; d′\_{t+1} = post(W(π·d\_t, ψ·y\_t, σ\_g s\_t)) = π·d\_{t+1}; r′\_{t+1} = P(π·r\_t, ψ·y\_t) = π·r\_{t+1}. Booking as effective: under PC\_g, the components at position π(x) correspond one to one, via κ\_g, to the components at position x, with the same values and thresholds, so the filtering \|c\| \> ε\_L corresponds position by position on the two sides, and W^ε\_num(π·d\_t, ψ·y\_t, σ\_g s\_t) = π·W^ε\_num(d\_t, y\_t, s\_t); under PC\_g^F the certification statuses correspond as well, and W^ε\_F commutes likewise; post commutes with π, and the induction runs as above. The entries of ℛ are relabeled one by one according to the field transformations of D18-5. ∎  
(2) The first two implications follow from (1) and the definitions. Strictness (the quantifier domain is the declared subgroup G4, the fixed model is the complete FLY model with K(C) as background, and the input protocol is restricted to {A, B, R}; \|G4\| = 4, element-wise equivariance in T2d):  
　\~ but not =, hence \~\_G but not ≈\_G: U (γ1 depths 1, 0 on K(A)) and V (1/2 each). The universal proof is a direct induction. Induction condition: the two states have the same total γ1 depth on K(A) and on K(B) respectively, and are identical position by position on α3, P and N. Rest: every γ1 position is multiplied by the same r1, the subset sums stay equal, and everything else is unchanged. Presenting A (likewise B): MBON\_γ1(A) and PPL1-α3(A) depend on γ1 only through the γ1 sum over K(A), hence are equal; the other MBONs are given by compartments that are identical position by position, hence are equal; each α3 position receives, from pre-states identical position by position, the same signed write and the same clipping, so the post-states remain identical position by position; γ1 shallowing at equal rate preserves the subset sums. Hence for every finite ABR history the full activity is the same beat by beat. Clipping may be kept, because the clipped α3 positions are at all times identical position by position between the two states. The multisets of element values of U and V differ, so the two lie on no relabeling orbit (T2f). Finite cross-check T2b (1093 histories).  
　≃ but not \~: Ua (α3 depths 1, 0) and Va (1/2 each); at the first presentation of A the full activity is the same, at the second it differs because of clipping (T2g); every g in G4 separates the two within length ≤ 2 (T2h), so ≃\_G but not \~\_G also holds.  
　Same actual activity but not ≃ (witness to the failure of the converse of ≃ ⇒ same actual activity): under the same actual signal the two states have the same full activity, and under another probe signal they differ (T1d).  
　Strictness of the two cross-convention arrows, \~\_G but not \~ and ≃\_G but not ≃: X (γ1 depth 1/2 on K(A)) and its image Y under (0 3)(1 2) (on K(B)): after relabeling the activities are exchanged, while at the fixed label A MBON-γ1 is 1 versus 2 (T2e).  
　Arrow-by-arrow summary: ≈\_G ⇒ \~\_G (U, V); \~\_G ⇒ ≃\_G (Ua, Va); = ⇒ \~ (U, V); \~ ⇒ ≃ (Ua, Va); ≃ ⇒ same actual activity (T1d); \~ ⇒ \~\_G and ≃ ⇒ ≃\_G (X, Y). Each of the seven arrows has a strictness witness.  
　Dependence on the input protocol: adding the overlapping odor C makes U and V separable at once (T2c); likewise Ua and Va (T2h). Rerun by semantics: the exact dynamical identity of U and V holds both under the original mechanism and under booking as effective (ε = 1/10) (T20d); under booking as effective, G4 satisfies process pairing and is equivariant (T20c).  
(3) The γ1↔α3 swap is an automorphism of the KC × compartment connection graph (every KC projects to every compartment), but the full activity of the two states under A differs, and the swap is not in the declared subgroup G4 (T2i). Not being in G4 implies only that it does not belong to the declared subgroup; further, the read-out commutation identity Φ(π·d, b, σ\_g s) = ψ·Φ(d, b, s) fails for every signal permutation σ\_g and every activity-coordinate permutation ψ: the value multiset of Φ(d, A) differs from each value multiset of Φ(π·d, s′) (s′ = A, B, C), whereas a coordinate permutation does not change the value multiset (T25c). Hence the swap is not in Aut. ∎  
Status: (1) is elementary and of the same type as the preservation theorem of \[S15\] (the special case in which source and target are the same instance); (2) and (3) are definitions and constructive witnesses. For unanswered item 9 of \[S1\], it provides one comparison relation and its layering, without claiming a unique answer.  
Theorem 18-3 (functional decomposition mediated by tendency)  
Under LW: (1) the pre-booking change Σ\_ρ c\_{ρ,x} depends on the complete state only through (d\_x, c\_x, Φ\_σ(s)\|N(x), s\|x); when there is in addition a position-wise post-processing LP, so does the change Δd\_x of the actual post-state; when the post-processing is general (cross-position), this item is issued only for the pre-booking change (X18-29, T21a). (2) For z ≠ x, changing d\_z can change the pre-booking change (under LP, also Δd\_x) only if it changes Φ\_σ(s)\|N(x). (3) Under LP, Aff⁰ is determined by the values of the tendency at the write positions: Aff⁰ = {x : post\_x(d\_x + Σ\_ρ c\_{ρ,x}(d\_x, c\_x, Φ\_σ(s)\|N(x), s\|x), d\_x) ≠ d\_x}. (4) Probing is writing: when the tendency is read with s while the write gate is open, d changes accordingly (T1g). Non-invasive reading of the tendency is defined by the actual post-state: after the tendency is read with s, the actual post-state is d′ = d, and where there is residue, additionally r′ = r; when components are nonzero but post-processing makes d′ = d (e.g. saturation clipping), the reading is also non-invasive; non-invasiveness does not require Act⁰ to be empty (T21b).  
Proof: (1) By D18-9, the x-th component of W reads only (d\_x, c\_x, y\|N(x), s\|x), and y\|N(x) = Φ\_σ(s)\|N(x); under LP the x-th component of post reads only (W\_x, d\_x), so the same holds for the actual post-state. (2) is the contrapositive of (1). (3) From (1) and LP. (4) By definition; the saturation example by computation. ∎  
Status: the read–transmit–write decomposition \[Lemma\]. It does not imply duty read-back: that Φ varies with d does not give the history condition of any particular write process; for read-back see Theorem 18-4. LW constrains only the pre-booking write; locality at the level of post-states requires LP, and the two are issued separately.  
Theorem 18-4 (read-back theorem)  
Let the hosting mechanism have a valid process-by-process decomposition on L. Read-back reports the three-way verdict of Definition 6′ and Note 6′-1 of \[17\] (8.2): effective difference certified / functional support established / effective difference not certified. The three columns are an evidence summary, not values of the main verdict.  
The read-back atoms (Appendix A.1 readback\_atoms) are listed separately: numerical support \[Supp\_act ∩ Avail ≠ ∅\], read certification cert\_read, path Path, HistCond\_F, Act\_F (including cert\_eff), the numeric difference, and item-by-item evidence for the four clauses of Definition 6′—same beat, same process, same target; bearing positions (the contrast changes only Trace-Response positions already left at that beat, with evidence of their formation and retention earlier than that beat); actual-route certification; same-beat action certification. The main verdict combines the actual evidence of the atoms according to D18 of \[S2\] and the original formula of \[S1\].  
Contrast family: the input to read-back is the frozen, complete set of contrasts—each contrast carries a contrast identity and the positions it changes, with their values; a joint contrast is listed explicitly as one entry, or is generated from the declared joint-validity predicate and then listed. The Cartesian product of per-position candidate values must not stand in for the declared family of jointly valid contrasts: the shorthand of per-position candidate values denotes only the individual single-set contrasts (Appendix A.1 contrast\_family), and the Cartesian closure is used only when it is explicitly declared as the family of jointly valid contrasts (histcond\_diff\_joint, T10d). Each contrast is first normalized to its actual difference set {i : s′(i) ≠ s(i)}—setting a position to its original pre-beat value does not count as a change—and validity, bearing positions, numeric difference and route are all computed on the actual difference set; different encodings of the same contrast state therefore give the same result (T22i). Contrast identities must be unique, and contrasts with duplicate names are rejected; the four clauses of a certified entry are stored directly (T22j). A contrast conforms to clause (B) of Definition 6′ only if its actual difference set contains only positions already formed in T with pre-beat depth \> ε\_L.  
Numeric difference: among the contrasts conforming to clause (B), if the largest absolute total-component difference crosses ε\_L, record “numeric difference crosses threshold”; if none crosses and the declared coverage is complete, record “numeric difference nonzero, below threshold” or “numeric difference zero” according to whether the largest is nonzero; if the declared coverage is incomplete, record “contrast domain insufficiently covered”, which is not read as numerically below threshold (T22e); in that case the certificate status lists separately “value of the disjunction of listed contrast certificates” and “declared domain not yet covered” (T22h). This is the numerical part of clause (C) of Definition 6′, not a complete certificate.  
Complete certificate: computed contrast by contrast for the same beat, the same process, the same target and the contrast identity—the witness of the k-th contrast is the three-valued conjunction of \[\|Δ\_k\| \> ε\_L\] and that contrast's four clauses (0 if any term is 0; ⊥ if there is no 0 but some ⊥)—followed by a three-valued disjunction over the contrasts (1 if any one is 1; ⊥ if there is no 1 but some ⊥). Certification is bound to the contrast that obtains the threshold-crossing difference and is not spliced across contrasts: when the threshold-crossing difference belongs to one contrast and the route certificate to another, there is no witness (T22c); when the difference and the certificate both hold on the same contrast, there is a witness (T22d); when only single settings are declared and none of the single-set differences crosses the threshold, there is no witness, even if the Cartesian product of per-position candidate values contains some other, threshold-crossing combination (T22a); when a joint contrast is validly declared and holds, there is a witness (T22b). The values of the clauses (evidence carried by the contrast itself takes priority, clause by clause; otherwise the common evidence of the same event is used):  
　Same beat: in model mode it is given by the construction of the contrast (the same law, the same pre-state) and recorded as 1; through an explicit evidence interface it may instead be recorded as 0 or ⊥.  
　Bearing positions: decided contrast by contrast on the actual difference set according to clause (B); recorded as 1 if the actual difference set contains only positions already formed with depth \> ε\_L, otherwise as 0.  
　Route: not inferred backward from the numeric difference, and bound to the bearing positions that the contrast actually changes. The route of the k-th contrast is taken from the path that starts at its actual difference set (intersected with Avail) and reaches this event—unchanged positions do not enter the start set: it is recorded as 1 when that path is 1, and as ⊥ when it is 0 or ⊥, since a path value of 0 does not exclude other material routes; any other declared valid actual route must carry its own certificate independently and is entered either through evidence carried by that contrast itself (T22g, T22d) or through an explicit evidence interface declared as applying in common to all contrasts (T17e, T18g). The aggregate Path (starting from all of Avail) is an independent atom of HistCond\_F and does not replace the contrast-by-contrast route: with c = 1 + x + z, x = z = 1, only x having a certified path, and the frozen contrast setting only z to zero, the aggregate Path = 1 and the single-set difference of z is 1, yet the route of that contrast is ⊥, which gives no certificate value 1 (X18-36, T22f). The contrast details record, contrast by contrast, the actual four clauses of each contrast; “Def. 6′ four clauses” takes the four clauses of the witness contrast, and summary default values do not stand in for the four clauses of the witness.  
　Action: Act\_F is the action atom of the same F event; external evidence can only make up for what Act\_F leaves unproven, never override its refutation. When Act\_F = 0 (explicit refutation) the clause takes 0; an external claim of 1 is recorded as “evidence conflict” and blocked on the original refutation (T18a); when Act\_F = 1 and an external claim is 0, a conflict is likewise recorded and blocked (T18e); when Act\_F = ⊥ (unproven), an explicit external certification of 1 can make it up, with the source recorded as “explicit certification” (T18d); when the external source explicitly gives ⊥, the source is recorded as “explicitly unproven” (T18h); when nothing is given, the source is recorded as “unproven” and the clause is ⊥ (T18c). Unproven, refuted and conflicting are listed separately in the atoms (“action source”, “evidence conflict”).  
　Witnesses from a different mechanism or from an independent R layer must additionally carry an event identity, a mode and a certification basis, and are listed separately in the atom “independent witness”; they are not merged into the four clauses, the complete certificate or the summary of this F event (T18i).  
Summary rules (consistent with the three-way verdict values of Definition 6′ of \[17\]): if the complete certificate is 1, record “effective difference certified”; otherwise, if the numeric difference is nonzero but below threshold (some valid contrast makes Δ\_tot ≠ 0 while none crosses ε\_L), or if HistCond\_F = 1 (nonzero functional support on a certified path in the sense of D15–D18 of \[S2\]), record “functional support established”; record everything else as “effective difference not certified”. “Effective difference not certified” does not override a positive determination that satisfies the original antecedents of \[S2\], and HistCond\_F = 1 is still recorded (T14f). When cert\_eff = 0, the complete summary is not given: with c = 1 + x, x = 1 → 0 and ε\_L = 1/10, the numeric difference 1 crosses the threshold; with Path = 1 but cert\_eff = 0, Act\_F = 0, the complete certificate is 0 and the summary is “functional support established” (T17a); with Path = 0 the route is recorded as ⊥ and the summary is “effective difference not certified” (T17b); when certification is unknown, the complete certificate is ⊥, and both the numerical evidence and the unknown are recorded (T17c); when all four clauses hold, it is “effective difference certified” (T17d). A nonzero valid finite difference is not in general equivalent to nonzero functional support in the actual state: inferring Supp\_act ∩ Avail ≠ ∅ from a difference via D12 requires the antecedent of (2)(A) (the contrast changes only available Trace-Response positions), and HistCond\_F further requires the remaining conjuncts of (2)(B); with c = s(j1)s(j2) and s = (1, 0), changing j2, whose depth is 0, gives a difference 1 \> ε\_L, but j2 is not an available Trace-Response position, so that contrast does not conform to clause (B), and the contrast on the available position j1 gives difference 0: HistCond\_F = 0, numeric difference zero, summary “effective difference not certified” (T14g).  
(1) Correspondence of Act: Act(ρ, Org(T)) under the declared semantics ⟺ ∃ x ∈ T, \|c\_{ρ,x}\| \> ε\_L (and at the F layer, additionally cert\_eff = 1). This coincides with the component criterion of \[S1\] and with Act\_F of D18 of \[S2\] (a restatement of definitions). Cancellation: two components +a and −a are each an action, so x ∈ Act^ε while x ∉ Aff⁰ (T3).  
(2) Relation between the two readings of history condition: let the law of ρ belong to the law class of D12 of \[S2\] (constant, linear, bilinear).  
　(A) Numerical support: if there is an effective-difference witness in the sense of Definition 6′ of \[17\] (four clauses: same beat, same process, same target; the contrast changes, singly or jointly, only Trace-Response positions whose depth in the pre-beat state of that beat is \> ε\_L and that have evidence of formation and retention earlier than that beat; the total-component difference recomputed under the unclamped mechanism is \> ε\_L; route and action certifications hold), then Supp\_act(ρ) ∩ Avail ≠ ∅. The proof uses only its clauses (B) and (C) (T4a).  
　(B) Extension to HistCond\_F: by the full conjunction of D18 of \[S2\], HistCond\_F(ρ) = \[Supp\_act ∩ Avail ≠ ∅\] ∧ cert\_read ∧ Path({(i, t) : i ∈ Avail\_act}, m), where the path, by D14 of \[S2\], starts from the true start set and reaches the event node of ρ only through edges with certification value 1; a path that exists only from other start points is not enough (T10b). The conjunction is three-valued: if any term is 0, HistCond\_F = 0; if no term is 0 and some term is ⊥, it is ⊥ (T10c); if every term is 1, it is 1. With read certification ⊥ and path 0, it is 0 (T14e).  
　(C) The converse fails, and the separation falls on effective writes: c = 1 + d/20, valid values \[0, 1\], d = 1, ε\_L = 1/10: the actual component 21/20 \> ε\_L is effective; with k ≠ 0, d available, and path and read certification equal to 1, HistCond\_F = 1; yet every valid contrast (joint ones included) gives a difference ≤ 1/20, and there is no effective-difference witness (T10a, T4b, T4c).  
　(D) Completeness of the certificate: the effective-difference certificate must be enumerated over the declared family of valid contrasts, and when joint contrasts are valid they must be included: c = 1 + (3/50)(a + b), a = b = 1, each single setting gives 3/50, and the joint setting declared as jointly valid gives 3/25 \> ε\_L (T10d, T22b); when the protocol allows only single settings, the joint difference does not enter the certificate (T22a). The program histcond\_diff verifies only the single-setting subprotocol; histcond\_diff\_joint and max\_contrast\_diff are used only when the declared family of jointly valid contrasts is exactly the Cartesian closure; both give only the numerical part of clause (C), and the complete certificate further requires item-by-item certification of same beat, bearing positions, route and action on one and the same contrast (preamble); the absence of a witness on an incomplete contrast domain is recorded as “contrast domain insufficiently covered” or “effective difference not certified”, and is not a negative determination.  
(3) Joint processes: when no valid process-by-process decomposition exists, the joint process is the unit; where the net change is zero but the evidence of physical action is not zero, Act is recorded as ⊥ (D9 of \[S2\]; unanswered in Section 8 of \[S1\]), and nothing is issued.  
(4) Entry into the F layer of \[S2\] via J₁₈: three conditions, each evaluated beat by beat (Appendix A.1 j18\_check, T23).  
　(A) An F candidate event table can be generated: a valid process-by-process decomposition exists, and every component and pulse of the runs of this paper is compiled from its raw fields into an event of the F layer of \[S2\]—event identifier, beat, target position, whether the carrier is inside B, the D12 numerical law (law class, read positions, coefficient, bias), the raw action certification cert\_eff and read certification cert\_read (taking 1, 0, ⊥, not merged in advance with the numerical threshold), the occurrence certification of pulses and whether they are out-of-boundary, and links to the evidence-path graph; the formation record Form and the out-of-boundary cause Mark are not filled in by this paper on the evaluator's behalf, but are registered by the evaluator of \[S2\] at the beat of occurrence from certified effective pulses and effective mechanism components, and at reading only entries whose formation beat is earlier than the current beat are used (D18-2; those formed only at the same beat are not counted, T23f). The numerical values entering the computation are normalized in both arms by the exact interpretation of \[S2\] (a decimal is taken as the exact rational given by its decimal representation, field\_final.exact), and the original-mechanism arm does not use floating-point arithmetic (T23k, T23l); whether the evidence graph is closed is declared by the evidence protocol and passed in, and an undeclared graph is not compiled—for an open graph with no path HDyn\_F is ⊥, for a closed one 0, and with a certified path 1 (T23n). The raw input is then first checked against D20 of \[S2\]—Structural Positions and event identifiers pairwise distinct, event times within the window, numerical laws and read positions valid, the path graph pointing forward, update values nonnegative, cert\_eff ≠ ⊥, the occurrence certification of pulses decided—and only input that passes is allowed to run F; where an action certification is undetermined (zero components included), the run is rejected and the R layer is reported separately as pending (T23a, T23b); an update value below zero leaves the domain (T23i). For signed depth to enter the F layer, a nonnegative encoding and a depth read-back must be declared (e.g. d = d⁺ − d⁻, T23j). Limits of applicability of the compilation: each component, after substitution of the activity, can be written in the law class of D12 (example: the *Drosophila* α3 write c = −ηa + ηb·Σ\_{K(s)} d\_γ1 is a linear law, T4e; a thresholded read-out, after substitution, does not belong to D12). Appendix A.4 is the evaluator field\_final.py of Appendix A.1 of \[S2\] (the text is identical word for word); the checks of this item and the evaluations in (B) and (C) call it directly (Appendix A.1 j18\_spec, j18\_check).  
　(B) The numerical trajectory of the original run is reproduced: one beat under the original-mechanism semantics of this paper is d′ = post(d + Σ\_ρ c\_ρ) (when there are no separately listed pulses, pulses count as components); one beat in 4.2 of the F layer of \[S2\] is d′\_F = d + Σ\_{ρ : \|c\_{ρ,x}\| \> ε\_L ∧ cert\_eff(ρ) = 1} c\_{ρ,x} (plus effective pulses). At every beat, every position must satisfy post(d + Σorig)\_x = d\_x + ΣF\_x, and the F-layer state must lie within the domain of D20 of \[S2\] (T14b). It is not enough that the laws belong to D12 and post does not change the post-state: with a constant component +1, cert\_eff = 0 and post the identity, the original-mechanism post-state exceeds the F-layer post-state by 1 (T10f, T23c); when cert\_eff = ⊥, F does not run and this condition is not evaluated (T23a, T23b); equal booked totals are not enough either: with initial state 1 and +1 booked on both sides, the original mechanism clips to the upper limit 1, while the F-layer post-state is 2 (T14a). Simplified form on the common domain: if the two sides have the same pre-state, with initial state and encoding matched; pulses (cert = 1, a\_p \> ε) and formation records are the same on both sides; the F-layer state lies within the domain of D20; and post changes the post-state on neither side at that beat—then this condition holds if and only if at every position the booked totals of the two sides are equal: Σ\_ρ c\_{ρ,x} = Σ\_{ρ : \|c\_{ρ,x}\| \> ε\_L ∧ cert\_eff(ρ) = 1} c\_{ρ,x} (T10g, T14c); when in addition every component has cert\_eff = 1, this reduces to “at every beat and every position, the sum of the nonzero subthreshold components is zero” (T4d). The simplified form is issued only on the listed common domain; a run in which clipping is triggered does not belong to the F layer (T4f).  
　(C) Witnesses and qualification are preserved: on the same valid event table as in (A), the evaluator of \[S2\] computes the per-event atoms Act\_F, Cond\_F, HistCond\_F and the Trace\_F, Part\_F, HDyn\_F, Q\_F of D19 (T23). Complete fields or equal Act\_F do not suffice to issue witness preservation: for a qualifying action without out-of-boundary cause, Trace\_F = 0 and Q\_F = 0 (T23d); a pure-read process has Cond\_F = 1 and Act\_F = 0, books nothing, and can still bear Part\_F (T23e); when an out-of-boundary pulse forms the element earlier than the reading beat, HistCond\_F and Trace\_F can be 1, and when it forms the element at the same beat, HistCond\_F = 0 (T23f); in a positive example on the same table the trajectory is reproduced and Q\_F = 1 (T23g), and the event atoms read back in this paper coincide with those reported by the evaluator (T23h). Theorem 2 of \[S2\] preserves the same-event witnesses of J(F): equality of state trajectories ((B)) does not by itself imply equality of evidence and qualification. When the conditions fail, only the R layer is entered (6.2).  
　(D) Residue: when there is a residue r and the F layer carries only d, trajectory equality compares the projection d that F can carry, not the complete state (d, r): the two complete states (0, 0) and (0, 1) have the same d projection, and when the write reads the residue, d is 0 versus 1 after one beat (T14d). Only when the residue and the write's reading of the residue are faithfully augmented into the F layer (r as positions of the F layer, with its reading in the D12 law class) are complete states compared; otherwise it must be declared that what is compared is the projection, and equality of projections does not imply equality thereafter.  
Proof:  
(2)(A) The component c of ρ depends only on the pre-state of the read positions R(ρ). Suppose the contrast changes only some available positions in T and \|c(σ′) − c(σ)\| \> ε\_L ≥ 0; then c is not a constant law, and at least one changed position j ∈ R(ρ) ∩ T. Linear law: since c changes, k ≠ 0, R(ρ) ⊆ Supp\_act, and j ∈ Supp\_act ∩ Avail. Bilinear law c = b + k·s(j₁)s(j₂): since c changes, k ≠ 0; when only j₁ is changed, the difference is k(s′(j₁) − s(j₁))s(j₂) ≠ 0, hence s(j₂) ≠ 0 and j₁ ∈ Supp\_act; likewise when only j₂ is changed; when both are changed, both are available, s(j₁), s(j₂) \> 0, and both lie in Supp\_act. (B) From the conjunction of D18 of \[S2\] and the definition of three-valued conjunction. (C), (D) From the listed computations. The value rules for the complete certificate and the summary in the preamble are definitions; the merging rule for the action clause guarantees that when Act\_F = 0 for the same event, the action clause is 0 for every contrast, so that the complete certificate is 0 whatever the external claim; computing contrast by contrast and then taking the disjunction guarantees that the difference and the certification in the certificate belong to the same contrast. ∎  
(4)(A) From the construction of J₁₈, the field requirements of D12, D13 and D16 of \[S2\] and the domain of D20; the check is executed by the evaluator of Appendix A.1 of \[S2\]. (B) The F-layer post-state in 4.2 of \[S2\] is the pre-state plus the components with Act\_F = 1 and the effective pulses; the original-mechanism post-state is post applied to the pre-state plus all components (pulses counted as components); comparing the two position by position gives the result; under the common antecedents post changes the post-state on neither side, and the difference of the two post-states is, position by position, the difference of the booked totals of the two sides; when in addition cert\_eff is 1 throughout, Act\_F = \[\|c\| \> ε\], and the difference is the sum of the nonzero subthreshold components. (C) From D18, D19, D21 and Theorem 2 of \[S2\]; the atoms and the qualification are computed by that evaluator. (D) From the listed construction. ∎  
Status: (1) and (3) are restatements of definitions and inheritance; (2) and (4) are the read-back work of this paper, with elementary proofs. (2) shows that on the law classes of \[S2\] the effective-difference certificate is one sufficient evidential entry point to the functional-support reading, and that the two are not equivalent; by Note 6′-1 of \[17\] the two stand side by side: failing to obtain an effective-difference certificate does not negate the strength dependence expressed by functional support, nor does it decide other modes of participation (occurrence, timing, position, direction). In the three columns the example of (C) is “functional support established”, and its atom HistCond\_F = 1 is not altered because the difference is not certified. The definitions and conclusions of \[S2\] are unchanged.  
Theorem 18-5 (local bearing of tendency and loop gain)  
Assume LW; fix the rest of the complete state σ\_{−x} and the signal s (quasi-static declaration), with the structural branch fixed over the interval under discussion (clipping, gridding, removal thresholds and topological switches are not triggered; residue dynamics take no part or have been included in the state).  
(1) Bearing: the one-beat change of x depends on the rest of the set only through a single curve, the tendency received by the position itself, ψ\_x (D18-13): if two configurations of the rest of the state give the same ψ\_x, the local dynamics of x are the same.  
(2) Stability: let the recursion at the position be δ ↦ F(δ) := δ + h·g(δ), g(δ) = G(ψ\_x(δ)) − γδ, with G and ψ\_x differentiable at the fixed point δ*. Then: if \|F′(δ*)\| \< 1, δ\* is locally asymptotically stable (sufficient); if \|F′(δ*)\| \> 1, it is unstable; if \|F′(δ*)\| = 1, the question is left to higher order and nothing is issued. F′(δ*) = 1 + h(L\_x − γ), L\_x := G′(ψ\_x(δ*))·ψ\_x′(δ\*); \|F′\| \< 1 is equivalent to 0 \< h(γ − L\_x) \< 2, and the step-size bound h \< 2/(γ − L\_x) must be kept together with L\_x \< γ, not replaced by L\_x \< γ alone.  
(3) Direction law: if ψ\_x is monotone in δ and w\_x is monotone in the local activity (possibly with thresholds), write m(ψ\_x), m(w\_x) ∈ {+, −} for their directions of monotonicity (+ for nondecreasing, − for nonincreasing; with thresholds only the direction of monotonicity is used, and no derivative is taken at jump points); the direction of the activity-mediated loop is m(ψ\_x)·m(w\_x): a positive loop—the deeper, the more it writes (continued writing, the rich get richer); a negative loop—the deeper, the less it writes (self-limiting). On a plateau of w\_x the loop does not change the post-state. This is a conclusion about direction, not about stability.  
(4) Two forms of bearing: under linear convergent read-out (MBON drive), ψ\_x(δ) = rest − w⁰δ, and the rest of the set bears only through the intercept (T6a); in a linear conductance network, ψ\_x(δ) = δV\_x/(1 + δR\_x), and the rest of the set bears through (V\_x, R\_x) (T6b).  
(5) Set level: when the rest of the set is not quasi-static, the local conclusions are only instantaneous. On one and the same fixed structural branch, if the read-out law and the write law are differentiable in a neighborhood of the fixed point d\* and the states in that neighborhood are feasible (do not leave the domain), the linear stability of the set's fixed point is determined by the spectral radius of J = I + h·(∂\_y W·∂Φ/∂d + ∂\_d W): ρ(J) \< 1 is sufficient, ρ(J) \> 1 means unstable, and ρ(J) = 1 is left to higher order. Two interventions must be kept apart: activity intervention ∂Φ\_U/∂d\_V (whether changing the depth of V changes the local activity at the write positions of U) and post-state intervention J\_UV = h·∂\_yW\_U·∂Φ\_U/∂d\_V (whether changing V changes the post-state of U); the latter can be zero while the former is nonzero (when the write is on a plateau, the gate is closed, or G′ = 0, T11b). Clipping, gridding, topological switching and residue dynamics must not be issued by a Jacobian containing only d; where there is residue, linearization must be carried out on the complete state (d, r).  
Proof: (1) By LW, Δd\_x depends on the rest only through (d\_x, y\|N(x), s), and with the rest and s fixed, y\|N(x) = ψ\_x(d\_x). (2) Only the differentiability of F at δ\* is used: F(δ) − δ\* = F′(δ*)(δ − δ*) + o(\|δ − δ*\|). If \|F′(δ*)\| \< 1, take q with \|F′(δ*)\| \< q \< 1; then in some neighborhood of δ*, \|F(δ) − δ*\| ≤ q\|δ − δ*\|, the neighborhood is invariant under F, and the iteration converges to δ\* at a geometric rate; if \|F′(δ*)\| \> 1, take 1 \< q \< \|F′(δ*)\|; then in some neighborhood \|F(δ) − δ*\| ≥ q\|δ − δ*\|, and points δ ≠ δ\* are pushed out of that neighborhood; F′(δ*) is obtained by the pointwise chain rule from the differentiability of G at ψ\_x(δ*) and of ψ\_x at δ\*; when \|F′\| = 1 the local behavior is determined by higher-order terms: u ↦ u − u³ is stable and u ↦ u + u³ unstable, both with F′(0) = 1 (T11a). (3) The direction of monotonicity of a composition of two monotone functions is the product of their directions; only monotonicity is used, not derivatives, so the result also holds with thresholds. (4) Linear convergence: MBON\_c(s) = Σ\_{i∈K(s)}(1 − d\_{ic}) is affine in the position; conductance network: with the other conductances fixed, the rest of the network seen from the two ends of tube segment x is a linear one-port network, which by Thevenin's theorem is equivalent to an open-circuit potential difference V\_x in series with an equivalent resistance R\_x; hence Q\_x = d\_x·V\_x/(1 + d\_x·R\_x), with V\_x and R\_x independent of d\_x. (5) When a multivariate map is differentiable at the fixed point, the expansion runs as in (2) with a norm adapted to the spectral radius (spectral radius \< 1 is sufficient; instability for \> 1 requires C¹ in a neighborhood, and the antecedent of this item already contains a differentiable neighborhood); the chain rule gives the entries of J and their relation to the two interventions. ∎  
Witnesses: T5 (a Tero-type single tube, with the rest of the set declared as a non-adapting background: in the two environments the initial-beat conductance, the flow rate and the first-beat change are all identical; at the initial state the instantaneous loop gain is L = 32/125 \> γ = 11/50 for R\_th = 1/4, and the tube segment vanishes; for R\_th = 4, L = 8/125 \< γ, and the tube segment is retained. The initial state is not a fixed point, so this is a witness consistent in direction with (2), not an instance of (2)); T5c, T5d (direction law of the γ1 self-loop toy); T6; T11.  
Status: (2) and (5) are standard conclusions on the stability of differentiable maps, and the conductance form in (4) is circuit theory; none of these counts as mathematical novelty. What Theorem 18-5 adds: it lands “what bears tendency” as a single curve ψ\_x, uniform across hosts, together with its loop gain; and it divides interventions between subsets into two layers, activity intervention and post-state intervention, the latter quantified on a fixed structural branch as the off-diagonal blocks of J. This is a conditional answer to “the general cross-host proposition on the bearing of tendency”: what is common across hosts lies at the level of ψ\_x, L\_x and J, while host differences fall on the functional form of ψ\_x (affine, fractional-linear, threshold), the form of G and the switching of structural branches.  
Theorem 18-6 (equivalent bearing: placing Marbach et al. 2023, a structural analogy)  
\[Published model\] Marbach et al. 2023, Equation 3: ⟨τ⟩ ≃ 4Q\_in(⟨a⟩)/(π⟨a⟩³) · 1/(1 + R(⟨a⟩)/R\_net), where Q\_in ∝ ⟨a⟩² is the tube's own peristaltic pumping and R\_net is the equivalent resistance that the rest of the network presents to the tube; the adaptation law is a continuous-time equation with time lag (Equations 1 and 2).  
Statement: (1) Correspondence of equivalent quantities: in the representation of this paper, the rest of the network enters the tube's shear in this equation only through R\_net (and pressure), i.e. the flow-division form of the tendency received by the tube itself, ψ\_x; the Thevenin form Q\_x = d\_xV\_x/(1 + d\_xR\_x) under a fixed-injection external source in this paper is the form under another drive; in both, the organizational quantity is R\_net (d\_xR\_x = R\_net/R). (2) Structural analogy: the paper's cues to fate fall into three layers—resistance regimes (R \< R\_net expected stable, R \> R\_net expected to shrink; tubes with R/R\_net \> 1 vanish, while nearly parallel neighbors with R/R\_net ≃ 1 persist), fixed-point stability of the given model (two stable fixed points, zero shear and the Murray steady state, with one unstable fixed point between them), and the empirical parameters of network integration (relative resistance and local pressure); and “the vanishing of one tube merges the neighboring tubes into a long tube whose resistance rises so that it loses stability”. Structurally, relative resistance corresponds to bearing (Theorem 18-5(1)(4)), fixed-point stability to the fixed-point condition of Theorem 18-5(2), and the avalanche to the incidence change of Theorem 18-9. Theorem 18-5(2) judges stability only at fixed points of a given discrete model; the instantaneous L\_x at the initial state is not a fate criterion (the initial state of T5 is not a fixed point); hence R/R\_net \< 1 and the instantaneous L\_x \< γ are not treated as two directly comparable sufficient fate criteria. (2) is only a structural analogy: the paper's model is a continuous-time system with time lag, and no item-by-item correspondence of its time evolution, dimensions, equilibria and linearization with this representation has been established, so it is not called a direct instance of Theorem 18-5(2).  
Proof: (1) By comparing Equation 3 with D18-13: with the rest of the network fixed, the tube's shear depends on the rest only through R/R\_net and the tube's own diameter; the time-lag variable τ\_s is the residue r of D18-2. ∎  
Status: a correspondence and structural analogy; no increment is claimed.  
Theorem 18-7 (conditions for the manifestation of hidden change)  
(1) Criterion for permanent hiding in the full activity (restricted class: no residue; LW; the full activity depends on the Trace-Responses through a linear drive, and the drive is a component of the activity; at every beat the actual post-states of the two arms are given by the same affine map—that is, the actual filtered write has a common affine form W^act\_j(v)\_x = α\_x v\_x + β\_x (under the original mechanism this is w\_x; under booking as effective it means W^ε\_num itself, without borrowing the original-mechanism slope), whose coefficients are the same in the two arms under common activity, and the post-processing is one and the same affine map post(v) = B\_j v + b\_j (identity, position-wise affine maps and permutations all qualify); A\_j := diag(α\_x) is defined by the actual filtered write. Non-affine post-processing such as clipping, component-wise gating that makes the sets of booked components differ between the two arms, and cases where the booked sets coincide but the filtered write is non-affine do not belong to this class; they are proved separately or excluded): σ \~ σ′ if and only if C\_{s\_k}B\_{k−1}A\_{k−1}⋯B₀A₀Δ = 0 ∀k along every history (Δ := d′ − d; B\_j is the linear part of the common post-processing at that beat, with B\_j = I for the identity; C\_s is the linear coefficient of the drive on the Trace-Responses under signal s). Omitting B\_j leads to misjudgment: when the post-processing swaps two positions and only x is read, the criterion with A\_j = I gives permanent hiding, whereas the two states are separable at the next beat (X18-30, T21c); with position-wise affine post-processing merged into B\_j, the criterion agrees with the actual runs (T21d); clipping is not covered by this item (T21e). Booking the same set of components fixes only the result of filtering and does not guarantee that the filtered write is affine: with the two components x² and 1 − x² of x, on the domain \[0, 1/4\] with ε = 1/10, both arms book only the latter; the original-mechanism total write x + 1 is affine, but the filtered write x + 1 − x² is not; the criterion with A = I and B = I/8 obtained from the original-mechanism slope judges permanent hiding, yet under booking as effective the readings at the next beat are 5/32 versus 19/128 (X18-35, T21f).  
(2) Manifestation through saturation: clipping makes the write non-affine in the position itself, and an H2 difference can manifest (T2g).  
(3) Conductance media: when the full activity includes flow rates and pressures, the conductance of every element with a pressure difference is determined by the full activity; an H2 difference can fall only on elements that have no pressure difference for any s.  
(4) Partial readings: on a linear time-invariant closed loop Δ′ = A\_clΔ with reading C\_ρΔ, permanent hiding holds if and only if Δ belongs to the largest A\_cl-invariant subspace contained in ker C\_ρ; checking k = 0, …, n − 1 suffices for the universal statement (T7a); nonlinear or switched closed loops require separate proof.  
(5) Parallel opposition: y(n) = 2(d\_N(0)r\_Nⁿ − d\_P(0)r\_Pⁿ); with equal rates and equal depths it is identically 0; with unequal rates and equal positive depths it is nonzero for every n ≥ 1 (T7b).  
Proof: (1) We first prove: if the two states have the same full activity at each of the first k beats, then Δ\_k = B\_{k−1}A\_{k−1}⋯B₀A₀Δ. Induction on j \< k: at beat j the full activity is the same, so the affine coefficients of the actual filtered writes of the two arms are the same, and the difference after booking is A\_jΔ\_j; the same affine post-processing makes the difference of the post-states B\_jA\_jΔ\_j. ⟸: suppose that for every history and every k the expression vanishes under C\_{s\_k}; induction on k: when the full activity is the same in the first k beats, Δ\_k is that product, and the difference of the full activity at beat k is C\_{s\_k}Δ\_k = 0, so the full activity at beat k is the same as well. ⟹: suppose the two states are exactly dynamically identical; then along any history the full activity is the same at every beat; by the lemma above, for every k, Δ\_k = B\_{k−1}A\_{k−1}⋯B₀A₀Δ, and the difference of the full activity at beat k, C\_{s\_k}Δ\_k, is zero, i.e. C\_{s\_k}B\_{k−1}A\_{k−1}⋯B₀A₀Δ = 0. Both directions use the same product containing B\_j, and use the affine coefficients only along the common prefix on which the full activity of the two arms is the same. (2) Clipping makes α\_x depend on d\_x, and the linear evolution in the induction fails; T2g gives the manifestation. The same holds when the filtered write is non-affine (X18-35). (3) When the pressure difference Δp\_x ≠ 0, d\_x = Q\_x/Δp\_x is read off from the full activity, so if the two states have the same full activity, these elements have the same conductance; a difference can remain only on elements with Δp\_x = 0 under every s. (4) The reading sequence C\_ρA\_cl^kΔ is identically zero if and only if Δ lies in ⋂\_k ker(C\_ρA\_cl^k), which is the largest A\_cl-invariant subspace contained in ker C\_ρ; by Cayley–Hamilton, A\_cl^n is a linear combination of I, A\_cl, …, A\_cl^(n−1), so checking k \< n suffices. (5) The two Trace-Responses shallow geometrically at rates r\_N and r\_P; substitute into the reading formula; with equal depth d \> 0, y(n) = 2d(r\_Nⁿ − r\_Pⁿ); with equal rates it is identically 0; when r\_N ≠ r\_P, t ↦ tⁿ is strictly increasing on \[0, ∞) (n ≥ 1), so r\_Nⁿ ≠ r\_Pⁿ and y(n) ≠ 0; the difference form uses no ratio and covers the case in which one branch has shallowing rate 0 (T25d). ∎ Note: U and V with clipping do not satisfy the antecedent of (1); their permanent hiding is given by the direct induction of Theorem 18-2(2).  
Status: \[Lemma, restricted class\]; a special case of the standard unobservability argument.  
Theorem 18-8 (the write law rewritten by history: law-layer Trace-Responses) \[Conditional conclusion\]  
Setting: D18-11; the law-layer component c\_x ∈ {0, 1} modulates the direct-read shallowing rate of position x: within one beat, the relaxation component of an α3 position is −(1 − ρ(c\_x))·d\_x, with c\_x taken from the pre-beat state (a new c written in this beat takes effect from the next beat, T8i); the activity-mediated write does not depend on c; the write of c is gated either by local activity (history-controlled: when the subset containing x is presented and PPL1-α3 \> θ\_c, c\_x ← 1) or by a clock (clock-controlled: set to 1 once k\_c beats have elapsed since the first write); Φ does not read c. We compare runs under two fixed histories: apart from c, the read-out-relevant initial complete states of the two arms agree—same d, same residue r—while c differs, and the signal history is the same; the residue recursion P(r, y) is the same in both arms and does not read c directly (the general form P(r, y) of this paper satisfies this). Models that allow c to affect the read-out through the residue are listed separately and compared through the actual readings of the two arms; (1) and (2)(A) are not applied to them.  
(1) Tendency null space: the two states have the same current full activity under every signal (T8a). The residue must be the same: with the same d and different r, if Φ reads r the readings separate at the first beat (X18-40, T24f); with the same initial residue and a residue recursion that does not read c, the readings are the same until the first difference in the target-depth relaxation component (T24g).  
(2) First component difference and actual post-state difference:  
　(A) First difference in the target-depth relaxation component: under a fixed history, the beat at which the two runs first show a difference in the relaxation component at a target depth position is exactly the first beat at which c still differs and some position x has pre-beat depth d\_x ≠ 0 with differing ρ(c\_x). Before that beat the two runs have the same d and the same readings beat by beat; the isolation conditions required for this are: the two arms have the same initial r and the residue recursion does not read c; the read-out law does not read c (Φ does not read c); the set-to-1 component of c writes only c and does not enter the writes of other positions; post-processing is position-wise and each position reads only itself (LP), so the difference in c is not transmitted to d through post-processing. The object of this item is the relaxation component of the target depth; if the first difference of the whole event is under discussion, the set-to-1 component of c must be counted—when in both arms x = 0, c is 0 and 1 respectively, and a strong gate sets c at the same beat, the relaxation components are both 0 while the set-to-1 components are 1 versus 0, so the first difference of the whole event falls first on c (T24d). The change of c itself (setting 0→1) also takes effect according to the actual recursion: under booking as effective, the set-to-1 component is booked only if 1 \> ε\_L, and when ε\_L ≥ 1 the setting does not occur (T24a).  
　(B) Actual post-state difference: a component difference does not in general become a post-state difference. The actual post-states of the two runs at x at that beat are post applied to their respective booked sums, and their difference is judged on the values of the two arms after actual post-processing. Under the following common conditions the actual post-state difference equals the component difference (ρ(c′\_x) − ρ(c\_x))·d\_x ≠ 0: post is the identity on the target component at the actual values of both arms (post(v)\_x = v\_x); the relaxation components of both arms are booked (original-mechanism semantics, or booking as effective with both components of absolute value \> ε\_L); the other booked terms at the same position are the same in both arms (T15c). When this identity antecedent is not satisfied, the result is the difference after actual post-processing of the two arms: with the same position-wise post(v)\_x = v\_x/2, without truncation or gridding, a component difference of 1/5 gives an actual post-state difference of 1/10 (T24e). Clipping can cancel the component difference: in the FLYC model with γ1 at 0, α3 depth 1/10 and c equal to 0 and 1, presenting A gives −11/80 versus −39/400 before clipping and 0 for both after clipping (T15a); booking as effective can discard it: at depth 1/10 under rest, the two relaxation components −1/20 and −1/100 both have absolute value ≤ ε\_L = 1/10, and the post-states of both arms are 1/10 (T15b). When no actual post-state difference appears, the c difference remains and can later generate component differences again.  
(3) Read-out distinguishability and retention of the difference: the single-position reading—the difference persists at x until the read-out beat—is one path of manifestation; it requires (A) that the read-out at some subsequent beat be distinguishable between the two states (the dependence of the read-out on x is not on a plateau at the values of the two states: under the saturating read-out min(d, 1/2), depths 1 and 3/4 both read 1/2 and the state difference does not manifest, T8j), and (B) that the difference not be canceled by clipping, gridding or a further overwrite before that read-out beat is reached. It is not a general necessary condition: the difference can be transferred by writes to other positions or to the residue and read out there, and the difference at the original position need not persist (X18-31, T24b). The general criterion is to compare the complete successors and the declared readings of the two runs (T8b, T8d and T8f all compare actual readings).  
(4) Overwriting: if at some beat the c of the two runs is actually set to the same value by the same write (strong write: PPL1-α3 \> θ\_c, with the set-to-1 components of both arms booked and not canceled by post-processing; under booking as effective with ε\_L ≥ 1 the setting is not booked and no overwriting occurs, T24a), and after that beat the complete mathematical states of the two runs are the same—d at every position (including the positions other than those where c differs), c, residue, the clock-controlled timer and the existence variables all the same—then thereafter the two runs are the same beat by beat (T15d; T8g is a finite verification for length ≤ 4). “No readable difference before” alone does not suffice to imply beat-by-beat identity after overwriting: a hidden state difference can be read in the future; a claim of future equivalence when the complete states differ requires separate proof. If it is claimed that the c difference stays permanently hidden from the start until after the overwrite, the readings at each beat before the overwrite must also be the same beat by beat (in this setting, if the depth of x is zero before the overwrite, then by (2)(A) there is no component difference). A c difference that is not overwritten can manifest late: for the same pair of initial states, after 6 beats of rest PPL1-α3(A) falls into (0, θ\_c\], and after a weak write the readings first separate at beat 9 (T15e). Nor can a strong write erase a state difference already produced: at depth 1/2 one beat of rest already produces a difference; a subsequent strong write makes c equal to 1 in both arms, yet the α3 depths still differ (T15f).  
(5) Weak write: (i) the write actually takes effect—the component is nonzero and is booked in the actual post-states of both arms, with post the identity on the target component or with the value after actual post-processing counted (the common antecedents of (2)(B)); (ii) PPL1-α3 ≤ θ\_c, so that c is not overwritten at the same time; (iii) for at least one subsequent beat, different rates act on a nonzero depth, and the resulting component difference satisfies the common antecedents of (2)(B) and so becomes an actual post-state difference; (iv) there is a distinguishable read-out satisfying (3), or the difference, after transfer, is distinguishable in the declared readings—when all four hold, the c difference manifests (T8d). Instead of checking the antecedents one by one, one may also compare the complete successors and declared readings of the two arms directly. When the antecedents fail, nothing is issued: under booking as effective with ε\_L = 1, the weak write is not booked, and the α3 depths of both arms are 0 (T24c).  
(6) Distinguishing history control from clock control: the two arms are fixed to the same training history and the same read-out, and the same blockade is applied (blockade of DAN within the consolidation window, which under both hypotheses removes the direct-write component of α3 and the gating input of the consolidation write, T8e); then in the history-controlled arm c is not written, while in the clock-controlled arm c is set to 1 under its conditions of actual effect—the set-to-1 component is booked (under booking as effective, 1 \> ε\_L) and not canceled by post-processing (when ε\_L ≥ 1 the setting is not booked, see T24a)—and the difference between the two arms passes only through c (T8f); without blockade, c is set to 1 under both hypotheses, and the two cannot be distinguished (T8h).  
(7) Organizational identity: if the declared subgroup G fixes the relevant positions (does not move the positions where c differs), or the value multisets of c in the two states differ, then two states with the same d and different c are not on the same relabeling orbit; otherwise c at symmetric positions with the same d may be exchanged by relabeling, and each case must be checked. An organization description that reports only d is a distortion when c bears the write law.  
Proof: (1) Φ does not read c, and (d, r) is the same in both arms, so Φ(d, r, b, s) is the same in both arms. (2)(A) Induction under the listed isolation conditions: when d and r are the same in both arms, the activity is the same; P(r, y) is the same in both arms and does not read c, so r′ is the same; the activity-mediated write does not depend on c, so the two runs write the same on pre-states with the same d, and the difference in c is not transmitted to d through read-out or post-processing; the relaxation component of x is −(1 − ρ(c\_x))·d\_x, and the difference between the two runs is (ρ(c′\_x) − ρ(c\_x))·d\_x, nonzero if and only if c\_x ≠ c′\_x and d\_x ≠ 0; the induction gives the beat of the first difference in the target-depth relaxation component; the first difference of the whole event additionally counts the set-to-1 component of c (T24d). (B) The actual post-states of the two arms are post applied to their respective booked sums; when post is the identity on the target component and the booked terms of the two arms differ only in the relaxation component, the post-state difference is the component difference; otherwise the result is the difference after actual post-processing (T24e); the counterexamples for clipping and booking as effective are by computation. (3) From the definition of read-out; the plateau example by computation. (4) The run equations are a function of the complete mathematical state and the signal (U1); after the overwrite beat the complete states are the same, so by induction the runs are the same beat by beat thereafter; the beat-by-beat equality of readings before the overwrite follows from (2)(A); the examples of late manifestation and non-erasure are by computation. (5) From (2) and (3): a weak write is actually booked without overwriting c; thereafter different rates acting on a nonzero depth generate a component difference, (iii) makes it an actual post-state difference, and (iv) makes it manifest as a reading difference. (6) The blockade removes components that are the same in both arms; the difference between the two arms comes only from the write gating of c (history control) and from the clock (clock control). (7) π changes only labels, not values; two states with different value multisets are not on the same orbit. ∎  
Status: representation of law-layer Trace-Responses and conditional conclusions; predictions for the real *Drosophila* are PC-1 and PC-2 of 9.2.  
Theorem 18-9 (changes in connection, overlap and subset relations) \[Conditional conclusion\]  
(1) Derived incidence: if the existence of a connection is a threshold function of the Trace-Responses (Inc(d) = {e : d\_e \> θ\_e}; effective subset K\_eff(s; d)), then changes in connections, effective overlaps and effective subsets are derived from d and require no additional state; Theorems 18-1 to 18-3 still hold with Inc(d), K\_eff(s; d) in place of the fixed b, K(s) (the read-out law and the write law are already functions of d). Neural side: depression to the limit at shared KC positions makes the effective overlap vanish, and the tendency at the overlap probe changes (T9a, T9b); conductance side: a tube segment reaching the shallowing limit is a change in derived incidence, and the flow rates of non-adjacent tubes change (T9c).  
(2) The remote effect of removal requires network conditions and boundary conditions. Assume a linear conductance network in which tube segment e = (u, v) reaches the shallowing limit and is removed, and let Q\_e be the flow rate in e from u to v before removal.  
　(A) Fixed injection: the same fixed node-injection vector is applied before and after removal (the injections within each connected component sum to zero, with one reference node chosen), and u and v remain connected after e is removed, so that the problem of injecting unit flow at u and withdrawing it at v has a solution. Then the change of flow rate in any other tube segment x is ΔQ\_x = Q\_e·T\_x(u→v), where T\_x(u→v) is the flow rate on x in that unit-injection problem after e is removed. Hence the flow rate of x changes if and only if Q\_e ≠ 0 and T\_x(u→v) ≠ 0; removal by itself does not imply a remote change (the bridge tube of a balanced bridge has Q\_e = 0, and its removal changes no flow rate, T12b). Tube-by-tube equalities in T12a, T16b.  
　(B) Fixed-potential and mixed boundaries: if some nodes are at fixed potential (kept at the same fixed potential before and after removal), the transfer T of the fixed-injection network does not apply: with the two ends at fixed potentials 1 and 0 and two parallel branches of conductance 1 each, removing one branch leaves the flow rate of the other at 1, whereas applying T predicts an increase of 1 (T16a). In this case T is replaced by the incremental response T⁰\_x(u→v) under the same boundary: in the network with e removed, the increments at fixed-potential nodes are taken as 0, and +1 and −1 are injected at whichever of u and v are free nodes (the injection at a fixed-potential end is absorbed by the boundary and not counted); the flow rate on x is then T⁰\_x(u→v). Antecedent: the network after removal is solvable under the original boundary, and the incremental problem is solvable as well (after e is removed, every connected component either contains a fixed-potential node or has incremental injections summing to zero). Then ΔQ\_x = Q\_e·T⁰\_x(u→v). When both ends are at fixed potential, T⁰ = 0 and ΔQ = 0 (T16a); on the Wheatstone network the formula holds tube by tube under four boundary settings of the fixed-potential, mixed and fixed-injection kinds (T16c). In the fixed-injection case with only one reference node, T⁰ = T, and (B) reduces to (A).  
　(C) Bridge disconnection: when e is a bridge, source–sink conservation is checked component by component. If after removal some component contains no fixed-potential node and its injections do not sum to zero, the steady state after removal has no solution, and the formula is not invoked (T16e); when u and v are no longer connected and the unit-injection problem has no solution, the formula is likewise not invoked, and removing a dangling branch with Q\_e = 0 changes no flow rate (T16d).  
(3) Generation of Structural Positions: runs take place within a fixed set of latent positions X̂; a new Structural Position must register three items: the existence variable e\_x (a component of d); the causal route (the generation law as a write component, e.g. when a position is full and the signal continues, e\_{x+1} = 1 is set along the edge—the minimal representation of Equation 10 of *The Mathematical Form of the Field*, T9d, T9e); and the identity of the new element (a label in X̂, already in the declaration before generation). Aut acts on X̂ and must commute with the generation law (under booking as effective, W^ε\_num, the generation components additionally require PC\_g; under the F-layer certified filtering W^ε\_F, additionally PC\_g^F), and the induction of Theorem 18-2(1) holds as before.  
(4) Distortion: changing b or X by redeclaration outside the run (e.g. deleting a vanished tube segment from the model and continuing in a new model) loses the historical dynamics of the organizational change—the change is no longer borne by action within the same run, which is an intrusion on the run; it must be represented within the run.  
Proof: (1) Inc and K\_eff are functions of d, and the proofs of the theorems use only the fact that Φ and W are functions of (d, r, b, s). (2) In the original network, e carries Q\_e. In the network with e removed, keep the same boundary and withdraw Q\_e at u and inject Q\_e at v (counting free nodes only; node conservation at fixed-potential nodes is not a constraint in the first place); the original node potentials satisfy conservation at every free node and are a solution of this problem; by the antecedent, this problem and the actual problem after removal are both solvable with unique flow rates (in a component containing no fixed-potential node, potentials differ only by a constant, which does not change flow rates). The difference of the two problems is the problem, on the same network with homogeneous boundary (fixed-potential increments 0), of injecting Q\_e at u and withdrawing Q\_e at v; by linear superposition, Q\_x(e removed) − Q\_x(original) = Q\_e·T⁰\_x(u→v). Under fixed injection there are no fixed-potential nodes, and with a reference node chosen, T⁰ = T, which gives (A). (C) From component conservation: for a component containing no fixed-potential node, summing its node conservation equations requires the injections to sum to zero. (3) Existence variables are components of d and generation is a component; π acts on X̂ and preserves the generation law (under booking as effective, PC\_g or PC\_g^F additionally preserves the filtered generation of the operator in use), so every step of the induction commutes with π. (4) By definition. ∎  
Status: representation and conditional conclusions.  
Theorem 18-10 (solvability)  
Antecedent: the declared operators (the read-out law Φ, the residue law P, each component of the write law W, the post-processing post, the generation law and the read-out ρ) can all be evaluated exactly on rational inputs in finitely many steps (e.g. given as explicit expressions with rational coefficients or as finite piecewise definitions). Under this antecedent, on the finite, rational, deterministic models of D18-1, every finite history has a unique run (under U1), and Φ, Aff and Act can be computed exactly in finitely many steps; dynamical identity of finite length can be checked by exhaustive enumeration; permanent hiding on a linear time-invariant closed loop can be decided within n steps; the conditions for F-layer compilation (Theorem 18-4(4)(A)) can be verified syntactically, component by component, for laws given by decidable expressions (explicit constant, linear and bilinear forms); read-back atoms, complete certificates and three-column summaries can be computed exactly on finite contrast families; the removal response of a conductance network with general boundaries can be computed exactly under the solvability antecedent; laws given by general functions are not guaranteed to be decidable. No general procedure is provided for universal identity and permanent hiding over infinite signal classes, in continuous time, or for nonlinear closed loops.  
Proof: By the antecedent, each beat involves finitely many evaluations; a finite history has finitely many beats; exhaustive enumeration over a finite signal table is finite; Cayley–Hamilton; exact elimination for linear systems with rational coefficients. Uniqueness and the finite rational domain do not by themselves provide an algorithm; the algorithm comes from the evaluability of the declared operators. ∎

11\. Counterexamples and Boundary Witnesses  
All examples are constructions of this paper, and each is verified item by item in Appendix B.2.  
　X18-1  Same depth multiset, different placement: under the same signal the activities and successors differ (*Drosophila* T1a; flow network T1c). A list of Trace-Response values does not determine the organization.  
　X18-2  Same full activity under the actual signal, different tendency: two states of a two-tube network have the same flows and pressures under the actual injection, yet with node 2 as the probe source their pressure differences are 2 versus 4/3 (T1d). Tendency is not an alias for the outcome of activity.  
　X18-3  Residue: with the same d and the same input, r = 0 and r = 3/4 give the activity sequences \[0, 0, 0, 0\] versus \[1, 1, 1, 1\] (T1e); outside the domain, r ≠ 0 after one step (T1f).  
　X18-4  Cross-convention: X and its image Y under (0 3)(1 2) are identical up to relabeling, yet their fixed-label tendencies differ (T2e).  
　X18-5  U, V: exactly dynamically identical, yet not equal and not in the same relabeling orbit (T2b, T2f); adding C separates them (T2c).  
　X18-6  Ua, Va: the same current tendency, yet clipping makes the second presentation separable (T2g); under the relabeling convention they are also separated by G4, element by element (T2h).  
　X18-7  The γ1↔α3 swap: isomorphic connection graphs, different full activity (T2i).  
　X18-8  Separation of readings on an effective write: c = 1 + d/20; functional support is established, yet the numeric difference does not cross the threshold (T10a).  
　X18-9  A path starting only from non-available positions: HistCond\_F = 0 (T10b).  
　X18-10  Joint contrast: the single settings give 3/50 each, the joint setting 3/25 \> ε (T10d).  
　X18-11  Omitting cert\_eff: the subthreshold sum is zero, yet the post-states of the two sides differ (T10f).  
　X18-12  Equal booked totals, different post-states: initial state 1, +1 on both sides, upper limit 1 (T14a).  
　X18-13  Residue projection: (0, 0) and (0, 1) have the same d projection, yet d differs after one beat (T14d).  
　X18-14  Changing a position of depth 0 gives a valid difference of 1, while HistCond\_F = 0 and the numeric difference is zero (T14g).  
　X18-15  Clipping erases the component difference: −11/80 and −39/400 both become 0 after clipping (T15a).  
　X18-16  Booking as effective discards the component difference: the post-states of both arms are 1/10 (T15b).  
　X18-17  A c difference that is not overwritten appears late: the readings first separate at beat 9 (T15e).  
　X18-18  A strong write does not erase a difference already produced: c is 1 in both arms, yet the α3 depths remain 291/800 versus 2463/4000 (T15f).  
　X18-19  Parallel removal under a fixed-potential boundary: the fixed-injection transfer T predicts +1, the actual change is 0 (T16a).  
　X18-20  Bridge disconnection: a dangling branch has Q\_e = 0 and the unit-injection problem has no solution (T16d); for a bridge between source and sink, conservation on the components fails (T16e).  
　X18-21  The numeric difference crosses the threshold but action is refuted: Path = 1, cert\_eff = 0, and the summary does not give “effective difference certified” (T17a).  
　X18-22  The numeric difference crosses the threshold but the route is missing: the route is recorded as ⊥ (T17b).  
　X18-23  An external action claim of 1 does not override Act\_F = 0 for the same event: the action clause is 0, the complete certificate is 0, and a conflict is recorded (T18a).  
　X18-24  Two examples with \|F′\| = 1: u ↦ u − u³ is stable, u ↦ u + u³ is unstable (T11a).  
　X18-25  The activity intervention is nonzero while the post-state intervention is zero (T11b).  
　X18-26  A saturating read-out keeps a law-layer state difference from appearing (T8j).  
　X18-27  Removing the bridge tube of a balanced bridge changes no flow (T12b).  
　X18-28  The total write commutes with relabeling but the filtered write does not: components on two positions x:(3/20, 1/20), z:(1/5, 0), ε = 1/10, the two labels swapped; original-mechanism post-state (1/5, 1/5), booked-as-effective post-state (3/20, 1/5) (T20a).  
　X18-29  Under the cross-position post-processing post(d)\_x = d\_x + d\_z, LW holds yet Δd\_x changes with z (T21a).  
　X18-30  The post-processing swaps the two positions and only x is read: a permanent-hiding criterion that omits the linear part of the post-processing misjudges (T21c).  
　X18-31  Difference transfer: the difference on x is cleared after one beat and moves to z; reading z separates the two (T24b).  
　X18-32  The Cartesian product of per-position candidate values is not the declared family of jointly valid contrasts: when only single settings are permitted, the single-set differences are 3/50 each, and the joint difference 3/25 may not be used as evidence (T22a).  
　X18-33  Difference and certificate mismatched: the threshold-crossing difference belongs to the joint contrast and the route certificate to a single-set contrast; they are not spliced (T22c).  
　X18-34  Setting not booked: under booking as effective with ε\_L = 1 the 0→1 consolidation component is not booked, and no overwrite occurs (T24a).  
　X18-35  The same filtered set, yet a non-affine filtered write: the components of x are x² and 1 − x², post = v/8, ε = 1/10; a permanent-hiding criterion built from the original-mechanism slope gives zero, while under booking as effective the next-beat readings are 5/32 versus 19/128 (T21f).  
　X18-36  The aggregate path is true but the path from the actually changed origin is false: c = 1 + x + z, only x has a certified path, the frozen contrast sets only z to zero, and certificate 1 is not given (T22f).  
　X18-37  A zero component with action certification undetermined: \[S2\] D20 rejects the F run (T23a); a qualifying action with no out-of-boundary cause: Trace\_F = 0 (T23d).  
　X18-38  The first whole-event difference falls first on the law-layer set-to-1 component (T24d); the position-wise post(v)\_x = v\_x/2 makes the actual post-state difference half the component difference (T24e).  
　X18-39  A pairing that preserves values and threshold but not certification: W^ε\_num commutes and W^ε\_F does not (T20f).  
　X18-40  Same d, different r: d\_x = 0, c is 0 and 1, initial r is 0 and 1, Φ = r; the readings separate at the first beat while the relaxation components are both 0 (T24f).  
　X18-41  Redundant encoding of a contrast: in {x: 1, z: 0}, x is already 1; unless the contrast is normalized to its actual difference set, the unchanged x is borrowed as the origin of a route (T22i).  
　X18-42  Inconsistent numerical interpretation: if the original-mechanism arm adds the decimal initial value 0.2 and the pulse 0.1 in floating point, it falsely reports that the trajectory is not reproduced (T23k).  
　X18-43  Closed-graph condition undeclared: with no path and an evidence graph that is not closed, HDyn\_F is ⊥; 0 may not be given by defaulting to a closed graph (T23n).  
Boundary witnesses (positive): universal induction for U, V and 1093 finite cross-checks (T2b); element-by-element equivariance over G4 (T2d); the FLY α3 write compiled into a linear law of \[S2\] (T4e); the Thevenin form (T6b); 20 cases of the incremental-response formula (T16c); positive cases of the complete certificate (T17d, T18f); an explicit route making up for an uncertified F path (T17e, T18g); process pairing and equivariance under booking as effective (T20b, T20c); dynamical identity of U, V under both semantics (T20d); position-wise affine post-processing absorbed into B\_j (T21d); non-invasiveness under saturating clipping (T21b); a valid joint contrast, and everything holding in full on one and the same contrast (T22b, T22d); the same contrast with a further, proven actual route (T22g); the three conditions for J₁₈ through the evaluator of Appendix A.1 of \[S2\]: pure read, Form earlier than the current beat and with a source together with an out-of-boundary Mark, positive cases on the same table and cross-checks of the atoms on both sides (T23e–T23h); nonnegative encoding (T23j); the two filtered-write operators listed separately (T20e).

12\. Breach Modes  
K18-1  Under U1, one labeled description with two different tendencies (contrary to Theorem 18-1(1)).  
K18-2  A g in the declared subgroup G that still satisfies the conditions of D18-5 while some step of the induction fails to commute (contrary to Theorem 18-2(1)); or U, V satisfying the induction conditions whose full activities differ on some ABR history; or an example that satisfies PC\_g and is not equivariant under W^ε\_num, or satisfies PC\_g^F and is not equivariant under W^ε\_F.  
K18-3  Under LW, an example in which changing d\_z (z ≠ x) leaves the local activity of x unchanged yet changes the pre-booking change; or, under LW and LP, an example in which it changes the actual post-state Δd\_x (contrary to Theorem 18-3(2)).  
K18-4  Within the D12 law class of \[S2\], an example in which an effective-difference certificate exists while Supp\_act ∩ Avail = ∅ (contrary to Theorem 18-4(2)(A)); or one in which the actual equation post(d + Σorig) = d + ΣF holds position-wise while the post-states differ (contrary to (4)(A)); or one in which, on the common domain listed in (4)(B), the booked totals of the two sides are equal position-wise while the post-states differ (contrary to (4)(B)); or one in which HistCond\_F has a conjunct equal to 0 and yet a value other than 0 (contrary to (2)(B)); or one in which Act\_F = 0 for an event while the complete certificate is 1 (contrary to the action-merging rule of the preamble); or one in which the complete certificate is 1 while its threshold-crossing difference and its certification do not belong to the same declared contrast; or one in which the three conditions of (4) are all 1 while the D19 qualification readings differ from those reported by the evaluator of Appendix A.1 of \[S2\]; or one in which an input with undetermined action certification is allowed an F run; or one in which a contrast-by-contrast certificate accepts a route certification that does not start from the actual difference set of that contrast; or one in which two encodings of the same contrast state receive different certificates.  
K18-5  Under LW and a quasi-static remainder, two sets of remainder states with the same ψ\_x for which the local dynamics of x differ (contrary to Theorem 18-5(1)); or, with G and ψ\_x differentiable and 0 \< h(γ − L) \< 2, a fixed point that is not locally asymptotically stable (contrary to (2)).  
K18-6  In Equation 3 of Marbach et al., the rest of the network affects the shear in the tube itself through a quantity other than R\_net (contrary to the placement of Theorem 18-6; this breaches only the correspondence, not that paper).
